# OC-LuaJIT roadmap

*Living document — extend as steps complete or plans change. Rationale for the decisions baked in here lives in [feasibility.md](feasibility.md).*

## v0 — Skeleton *(done, 2026-09-01)*

- [x] Feasibility study with source-verified findings ([feasibility.md](feasibility.md))
- [x] GTNH ExampleMod1.7.10-based project skeleton, fresh git history
- [x] Architecture stub registered via `Machine.add`, visible in shift+right-click CPU cycling. (Its original LuaJ-style non-persistence premise was disproved by Track P and the class comment has been corrected; the stub has since been replaced by a `NativeLuaArchitecture` subclass, 2026-09-15, which has run in game since 2026-09-16.)
- [x] Watchdog research + standalone prototype **built, run, and validated** (0.025 ms interrupt of compiled loops with CHECKHOOK; stock build provably can't; ~0% tax on real work — see [watchdog.md](watchdog.md))
- [x] Native performance comparison on real hardware ([../bench/results-2026-09-01.md](../bench/results-2026-09-01.md)): SHA-256 **41×**, matmul 17×, mandelbrot 14×, trampoline 16×, geomean 4.6× (≈6.7× with the strings fix) vs Lua 5.3; interpreter-only floor 2.5×
- [x] First `./gradlew build` verified green (JDK 17 daemon; OC `1.12.58-GTNH:dev` resolves; stub compiles against the real OC API)

## v0.5 — ocelot-brain harness *(the development environment)*

Adopted 2026-09-01 after evaluation ([research/ocelot-brain.md](research/ocelot-brain.md)).
**This inverts the previous plan.** v1 assumed the JNI bridge would be validated
in-game; it is validated here instead, and Minecraft is touched only at v1's
final line. The reason is epistemic rather than convenience: this project's
standing weakness is that every verification claim was one step removed from
reality, and this closes that gap for the machine layer — where all the
remaining risk lives. Minecraft was never going to give us a differential
oracle against real Eris. ocelot-brain does. **Superseded -- 2026-10-03:** the ordering was overtaken: Minecraft has been used per fix since 2026-09-16 ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md) T1-T9; v1's validate-in-the-harness row). The harness can host the differential oracle; the comparison itself is the open differential-oracle row below.

- [ ] **The false-green guard, before anything else runs.** ocelot-brain sets
  `includeLuaJ = !isAvailable`, so a failed native load silently substitutes
  LuaJ — which has no Eris, so every persistence test then passes **vacuously**.
  This is the single failure mode that manufactures false confidence. A shared
  harness base class asserts the architecture is what we think it is and dies
  loudly otherwise. A base class, not a convention. **Status 2026-10-03:** Half done, and still a convention. `guard()` (`test/native/OcljSmoke.scala:1614-1705`, called at `:4019` before any persistence milestone) exits 3 on `!isAvailable`, `includeLuaJ`, a non-`NativeLuaArchitecture`, the wrong pinned class, or a fingerprint (`_OCLJ_NATIVE`, `jit`, `eris`, LuaState class) that contradicts `ocljit.native`. The other harness entry point does not: `CensusOs.scala:275-292` only prints a `!! ... DIFFERENT VM` line on a mismatch, skips the check if the architecture is still null after its settle loop, and its exit status (`:425`) reads only panic text and `lastError`; there is no shared base class, and `CensusOs` keeps its own copies of `luaOf`/`evalLocked`. A third gap (2026-10-03 review): `guard()` runs once, on the first machine. No restored machine is checked against the pinned class: the `stk` and `fi` restores (`ws3`, `ws4`) get no fingerprint at all, and the f-block's second computer only an indirect one, through `acc-1r` (`:5448-5458`), which is a milestone rather than a refusal and is skipped on stock. **Status 2026-10-10:** Now also the one CensusOs task. From the MineOS row it takes the verdict gap: `CensusOs.scala:417` scores only `PANIC` and `not enough memory`, and the 2026-10-03 MineOS run on ours reads `CENSUS PASS` with `stack traceback` twice on screen (`bench/runs/2026-10-03-ramscale/logs/census-mineos-D.log:64`, `:78`, `:106`), the fail-first case for a traceback check. From the MineOS-measurements row it takes a save step and the input injection that opens a window (`CensusOs.scala` has no save, persist or signal code). The census pin gap is narrower than written above: `smoke-test.sh:598-616`, which `census-os.sh` always runs through, fails a census on the `!!` line and on a missing or contradicting `_OCLJ_NATIVE` marker, and a null architecture prints no marker (`CensusOs.scala:321-331`); what is missing is a refusal in CensusOs's own exit (`:425`). Restores are still unchecked (`expectedArch` is read only at `OcljSmoke.scala:1625` and `:3981`); `vmFingerprint` (`:1596-1612`, d9080d4) can serve a restore check. A shared base class means a third source on the scalac line (`smoke-test.sh:394`). ocelot-brain's `includeLuaJ` is `!isAvailable || luajRequested` (its `LuaStateFactory.scala:29`), not `!isAvailable`; `guard()` refuses either (`OcljSmoke.scala:1615`, `:1619`).
- [x] **Run the smoke suite on OUR architecture, not only on the dropin — DONE 2026-09-16.** Every persistence, deadline, RAM-cap and bytecode-gate milestone had been measured with `NativeLua52Architecture` over a substituted `libjnlua52`; `OcljSmoke` pinned that class unconditionally and its `guard()` refused `ocljit.native=additive`. So the port was proven to BOOT and never to SAVE — and that is not a quibble, because `LuaStateLuaJIT` redeclares the five natives `PersistenceAPI` drives (`lua_dump`, `lua_pushbytearray`, `lua_tobytearray`, `lua_next`, `lua_rawset`) into our own JNI symbol family. The suite now derives the architecture from the same switch as the native, and the additive arm reports **32 checks, 0 failures** (`bench/runs/2026-09-16-additive-smoke/`). Two silent defects fell out: `setArchitecture` rejects any class absent from `MachineAPI`'s registry, so `register()` is required rather than optional as the adapter claimed; and `m2-persist-flush-observed` was gated on the literal `"luajit"`, skipping it for the shipped shape. The fingerprint now carries `class=`, which is the only discriminator between the dropin and additive arms — same VM, different JNI family, identical `_OCLJ_NATIVE` marker.
- [ ] **Pin ocelot-brain: a commit check and jar checksums** -- retitled 2026-10-10 (the user's decision: no submodule, no vendored copy); the original row: Vendor ocelot-brain at a pinned commit. No published artifact; consumed as
  a git submodule + sbt composite build, with three dependency jars fetched by
  URL from asie.pl — vendor those too. MIT, compatible with ours. **Status 2026-10-03:** Not vendored. There is no `.gitmodules`. `build-brain.sh:52-53` and `smoke-test.sh:309` take whatever checkout `$OCLJ_BRAIN` names, and with it the OpenOS floppy, the BIOS and the `machine.lua` the harness boots. The pin is only the comment `verified at 0.24.2 / e98a5b2` at `smoke-test.sh:123`, and the three asie.pl jars are still fetched at run time with no checksum (`build-brain.sh:93-95`, `smoke-test.sh:360-362`). The sbt composite build in the plan was replaced by `build-brain.sh`, which runs scalac 2.13.11 directly on JDK 8 because ocelot-brain's sbt 2.0.7 needs JDK 11+. **Status 2026-10-10:** Decided by the user: no submodule and no vendored copy, so ocelot-brain's licences and its three tracked 32-bit jnlua DLLs stay out of this tree. What remains is a pin check and checksums. `smoke-test.sh` and `build-brain.sh` should refuse when `git -C "$OCLJ_BRAIN" rev-parse HEAD` is not e98a5b2, unless an explicit override is set; the local checkout is at e98a5b2 and clean, so the pin holds today by convention. Both `get()` helpers should check a sha256 for each of the 17 jars they fetch, 14 from Maven Central and 3 from asie.pl (`smoke-test.sh:338-362`, `build-brain.sh:68-95`); today they test only that a file is non-empty. Each check is to be shown refusing first, on a wrong commit and on an altered jar. A checksum catches a changed jar, not a vanished host. `smoke-test.sh:123` and `:329` still name `sbt compile` rather than `build-brain.sh`.
- [x] ~~Split `LuaJITArchitecture` into a host-agnostic core plus two thin
  shells (~60 lines each).~~ **DONE 2026-09-15, and there is no shared core:
  there is nothing left to share.** The plan assumed we would implement
  `Architecture` and factor out what the two hosts have in common. We do not
  implement it at all. `NativeLuaArchitecture` is a `public abstract class` in
  both OpenComputers and ocelot-brain whose **only** abstract member is
  `factory()`, and each host's own three VMs are one-line subclasses of it; so
  are ours. The four divergences the plan enumerated — `recomputeMemory`'s
  body, the memory getters, the NBT write, the imports — are all **inherited**,
  which is why they stopped being our problem. What each adapter supplies is a
  `LuaStateFactory` subclass: `version()`, `create()`, `openLibs()`.
  `version()` is the load-bearing one — it determines the library filename the
  host's own loader hunts for, which is why the additive artifact is named
  `libjnluajit52-*`. See [research/shipping-model.md](research/shipping-model.md),
  "The port is a subclass, not a port".
  - harness adapter: `test/native/OcljArch.scala` — **runs**; AxisOS boots to
    `localhost login:` on it (`bench/runs/2026-09-15-census-axisos-additive/`)
  - mod adapter: `src/main/java/io/github/astronfo/ocluajit/arch/` — written
    and compile-checked against the pinned OC jar; **not run**, that needs
    Minecraft. **2026-10-03:** it has run in game since 2026-09-16 ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md) T1-T9), on OC 1.12.61-GTNH (the first in-game run row) and 1.12.64-GTNH (T7's instance, `bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md:781`).
- [ ] **Bound the `@Mod` OpenComputers dependency.** We now subclass
  `li.cil.oc.server.machine.luac.NativeLuaArchitecture` and `LuaStateFactory`,
  OpenComputers' implementation classes, pinned only at COMPILE time. The `@Mod`
  string is `required-after:OpenComputers;` with no version range, so an
  OpenComputers whose shape changed gives a `NoSuchMethodError` at class load —
  a startup crash for the player, not a build failure for us. Not guessed at:
  a malformed FML range stops the mod loading outright, and nothing here can run
  FML to validate one. Settle it in `runClient`. **Status 2026-10-03:** Unchanged at `OCLuaJIT.java:16`, and nothing in `src` catches a `LinkageError`. The premise that nothing here can run FML is out of date: the jar has loaded through Forge on OC 1.12.61-GTNH (roadmap:113) and 1.12.64-GTNH (T7, `bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md:781`), against the 1.12.58 compile pin in `dependencies.gradle:39`. A candidate range can therefore be tried in that Prism instance rather than in `runClient`. An interim `try/catch (LinkageError)` would have to enclose `:59` as well as `:67`, because `LuaJITStateFactory` extends OC's `LuaStateFactory` (`LuaJITStateFactory.java:48`) and touching `INSTANCE` already links against OC. The surface is wider than the two classes named above (2026-10-03 review), though most of it does not bind at startup. `LuaStateLuaJIT` extends jnlua's `LuaState` (`LuaStateLuaJIT.java:30`) and is built by the factory's throwaway state inside `OCLuaJIT.java:59`, so a change there is the same startup crash. `LuaJITArchitecture` also uses `OpenComputers`, `Settings`, `SaveHandler`, `NativeLuaAPI` and `PersistenceAPI` (`LuaJITArchitecture.java:12-20`) and `Machine.State` (`:409-412`), and reads the private `apis` field by reflection (`:445`). By the JVM's lazy resolution (a reading, not tested) those bind when a LuaJIT machine is constructed (`resolveApis()`, `:94`) or saves and loads (`:492`, `:566`), and OC catches every Throwable at all three (GTNH `Machine.scala:137`, `:809`, `:891` in the 1.12.55 sources): a computer that will not start, or a save that is logged and dropped. No try/catch around `:59`/`:67` reaches those; only a version range covers them. **Status 2026-10-10:** Unchanged at HEAD (`OCLuaJIT.java:16`; no `LinkageError` catch in `src`). Takes over the OC-version-pin open question: whether the compile pin moves from 1.12.58-GTNH (`dependencies.gradle:39`) to the 1.12.64-GTNH the game runs (`OpenComputers-1.12.64-GTNH.jar`, verified 2026-10-10). A lower bound refuses only an older OpenComputers; only an upper bound refuses a newer one whose classes changed shape, and it also refuses compatible later releases, so whether to set one is the user's decision.
- [x] Pin `NativeLua52Architecture` for every comparison. **Three languages are
  in play**: ocelot defaults to 5.3, GTNH ships 5.2, we are LuaJIT 5.1 +
  `LUA52COMPAT`. Any 5.3/5.4 result is inadmissible. **Done -- verified 2026-10-03:** `OcljSmoke.scala:1587-1589` derives the pinned class from `ocljit.native`: `NativeLua52Architecture` for the dropin and stock arms, and the 5.2-class `OCLuaJITArchitecture` for additive. `:3981` sets it, and `guard()` dies on a class mismatch (`:1625`) or on a stock `_VERSION` without 5.2 (`:1699`). Every in-machine stock baseline since `bench/results-in-machine-2026-09-03.md` (cell A) is PUC 5.2. `CensusOs.scala:196-210` defaults to 5.2 and runs 5.3 only as a labelled baseline arm, though its mismatch check only prints (see the false-green item). Outside the machine, `bench/run.sh` and `bench/oc/run-standalone.sh` (`:66-67`, "lua5.3 stands in for the PUC 5.2") still baseline on lua5.3 (tracked since 2026-10-03 in the benchmark-upkeep row). **Status 2026-10-10:** "GTNH ships 5.2" is wrong: GTNH's default CPU is Lua 5.3 (OC `application.conf:232`, `defaultLua53`; the user's `config/OpenComputers.cfg` sets `defaultLua53=true` and `enableLua54=true`, verified 2026-10-10). The pin stands, since 5.2 is the language this VM implements; but a GTNH player's default CPU, and the one a player compares against, is the 5.3 CPU (the 5.3-ecosystem row).
- [ ] **The differential oracle.** Same host, same workload, same save point:
  persist with real Eris on PUC 5.2 and with `eris_lj` on LuaJIT, then compare
  accept/refuse decisions and post-restore observables. This is the strongest
  test available to this project, and nothing else can produce it. **Status 2026-10-03:** Both arms exist, but nothing compares them. The smoke suite runs the same OpenOS persist/restore workload on `OCLJ_NATIVE=stock` (PUC 5.2, real Eris, OC's kernel; 41/0 at the 2026-10-02 arena gate, the allocator row) and on ours: the f1-f7 round-trip, the mid-for-in save (fi-1..3) and the mid-sync-call save (stk-1..4). But each arm checks its own expectation in a separate run (`stk-2-stock-control-small-second-blob`, `OcljSmoke.scala:1036`, and the per-arm stk-3/stk-4 ids at `:1058-1060` and `:1063-1065`; `fi-2/fi-3-stock-control-*`, `:1209-1210`), and nothing compares accept/refuse decisions or post-restore observables (screen, signal queue, `lastError`) between the two. The one recorded accept/refuse divergence, M4.4's empty-perms refusal (the M4.4 row, Track P), came from a standalone rig that is not in the tree. **From the 2026-10-03 review:** a comparator has to hold the kernel fixed as well. The stock arm runs OC's kernel (`smoke-test.sh:222-225`) and ours the patched one, and the additive architecture cannot run OC's kernel at all (the `W6b` row), so a stock-against-additive diff mixes the kernel and the architecture's save shape (the bundled roots, the `_stack` row) with the VM. The pair that holds both fixed is the dropin native on OC's kernel against stock, since both pin `NativeLua52Architecture`; but that arm is not maintained: its native was last built 2026-09-17, and its last run failed f3/f5/f7, fi-1..3 and stk-3/4 inside tick-counted caps under the standing hook ([research/hook-vs-jit.md](research/hook-vs-jit.md):430-441). It needs a fresh dropin build, and `OCLJ_JIT=off` or the `W6b` row's wall-based caps, before it can serve. Otherwise, record which divergences are the kernel's and which the architecture's. **Status 2026-10-10:** Still nothing diffs the two arms. The dropin is no longer the 2026-09-17 build: it is rebuilt and gated at every wall stage on the watchdog kernel (`bench/results-wall-2026-10-04.md:54-59`), 63/0 at a4994e6 beside stock's 47/0 (`bench/results-reserve-2026-10-05.md:292`), so the fresh build asked for above exists. Only its run on OC's kernel is missing, last made 2026-09-29: every October log on OC's kernel is `native=stock`. The wall-based caps that run needs moved with the `W6b` row's caps to the run-until-a-condition row; `OCLJ_JIT=off` remains the way round them.
- [ ] Tests are "run until a condition, with a timeout" — never "step N ticks".
  Lua runs on a thread pool against a wall clock, so tick-counting tests flake,
  and a flaky persistence test is worse than none. Saves cannot tear:
  `Machine.save` and `Machine.run` share a lock. **Status 2026-10-03:** Half done. The boot wait and `d` now wait on their condition with per-kernel caps (`OcljSmoke.scala:4239`, `:4285`) and most milestone loops are condition-plus-cap, but `f3-restore-counter-continues` still steps a fixed 160 ticks and then asserts the counter advanced (`:5350`, `:5430`) -- the shape `d` had before its fix -- and every cap still counts polls (mostly 25 ms; 10 ms in `stk`, 30 ms in the a/b start-up loops at `:3990` and `:4005`) rather than machine uptime, which is how the 2026-09-19 load matrix (the `fires=0` contention row) and the 2026-09-29 stock-kernel arm ([research/hook-vs-jit.md](research/hook-vs-jit.md):439) lost runs to caps closing early. Fixed tick counts with no condition at all also remain in the settle loops before several milestones read the screen (`:570`, `:1248`, `:1332`, `:1469`, `:3632`). The lock half holds as written: `save` and `run` both take `Machine.this` in ocelot-brain (`Machine.scala:758`, `:907`) and GTNH OC (`:821`, `:983`). **Status 2026-10-10:** Takes over the `W6b` row's poll-counted caps: the boot cap (`OcljSmoke.scala:4224`), `d`'s (`:4282`), k1's 600-poll wait (`:4506`), p0's 800-tick wait (`:4655`) and the capped loops that row lists. This row now owns every cap; the `W6b` row keeps W6b's absolute `dt < 20.0` ms bound (`test/native/wd_test.c:319`), and the watchdog-delivery-under-load row and the differential-oracle row's OC-kernel arm wait on this one. Unchanged at HEAD: all 34 `Thread.sleep` poll sites count polls, and no load flag or host factor exists. New since the note above: the capacity probe adds a fixed 40-tick settle before it reads the screen (`:3483`); its fixed 400-tick idle window (`:3425`) is a measurement, not a wait. Each new cap is to be shown closing on a stalled machine.
- [ ] **Two one-run measurements, both left open by the MineOS census** ([research/mineos-census.md](research/mineos-census.md)): (a) boot MineOS on ocelot-brain with the machine's RAM set to its own declared floor (2048 KB) and try to save -- this either promotes "the save may not fit" to a ranked defect or retires it, and it is the only census row whose fix would be in OUR C code (the write buffer doubles through the host allocator and `lua_pushlstring` then copies it again, so peak is live + buffer + blob); (b) capture a save point with an application WINDOW open -- the stock baseline covers only an idle desktop, and three of MineOS's four interesting shapes exist only once a window is open. **Status 2026-10-03:** (a) is settled from source without the run, and E4 retires as a refusal: stock OC (`NativeLuaArchitecture.scala:398-400`), ocelot-brain (`:400`), our adapter (`LuaJITArchitecture.java:496-500`) and the harness's (`OcljArch.scala:322`) all lift the cap to `Integer.MAX_VALUE` for the whole persist and reapply it after, so the eris write buffer (`eris_lj.c:249-254`) is charged but cannot be refused -- the census's reconciliation assumed stock OC enforces the cap there, and it does not. What remains is a transient host-memory peak of live + buffer + blob (`eris_lj.c:3119` copies the buffer), a candidate optimisation rather than a defect. MineOS has booted at the 2048 KB floor on our native (`bench/runs/2026-09-15-census-mineos/census.txt`: 14000 ticks, running, peak 3053003 B real; **2026-10-03:** that was the dropin arm under OC's own 5.2 architecture class, and it never reached the desktop -- on the shipped architecture MineOS dies at boot, the MineOS row, so (a) and (b) wait on that), but `CensusOs.scala` never saves, so no save at the floor has been measured, and (b) has never been run. **Status 2026-10-10:** Merged 2026-10-10 into the false-green-guard row: the CensusOs work the measurements need, a save step for both and input injection to open a window for (b) (`CensusOs.scala` has no save, persist or signal code). The measurements stay here. (a)'s retirement as a refusal holds after the wall work: the shim still refuses only when `total - used < delta`, in both its modes (`native/lj52shim.c:410-417`, `:477-489`), and the persist's `Integer.MAX_VALUE` cap reaches the shim (`LuaStateLuaJIT.java:70-72`). (b) on the stock arm waits only on the CensusOs work; on ours it also waits on the MineOS row.
- [x] **Mirrored host state is an atomicity obligation.** MineOS keeps a full Lua-side framebuffer mirror and renders only the diff, so its `currentFrame*` is a cached belief about the screen used to SUPPRESS work. Not a defect today -- stock OC and ocelot-brain satisfy it by construction (one lock, both halves in the same NBT) -- but our architecture must never break it: one lock, one instant, never restore one half without the other, never pair a stale component NBT with a fresh blob after a failed or retried save. Measured consequence of breaking it: ~64% of the desktop missing permanently, `isRunning=true`, `crashes=0`, `lastError=null`, and it re-saves cleanly. **Done -- verified 2026-10-03:** holds by construction and stays a standing rule rather than a task. `LuaJITArchitecture.save` runs only inside OC's `Machine.save`, under the `Machine.this` lock that `run()` also takes (`Machine.scala:821`, `:983`), and the screen's own save first pauses every computer on its network under that lock, waiting out any running execution, before it schedules `_buffer` (`TextBuffer.scala:482-490`) -- OC's mechanism, inherited unchanged. A persist that raises a Lua error removes `state` (`LuaJITArchitecture.java:550`, `:556`), so `Machine.load` closes the machine (`:774`, `:815-817`) rather than pairing a screen with a stale blob, and `src/main/java` has no deferred, async or retried save. No test targets the rule, so re-check this row if save ever gains such a path.
- [ ] Record the three ocelot-brain defects that will otherwise be misread as
  ours: `Integer` signal loss (`Machine.scala:800-814` has no
  `java.lang.Integer` case and falls through to `setByte(-1)`),
  `new Memory(ExtendedTier.Creative)` throwing on insert, and `brain.conf`
  replacing rather than overriding config. **Status 2026-10-03:** Recorded in research, not in the harness. All three are in [research/ocelot-brain.md](research/ocelot-brain.md) (:36, :52) and still hold against the current checkout: `convertArg` keeps `java.lang.Integer` (`Machine.scala:291`) and the signal save falls through to `setByte(-1)` (`:814`); `Memory.amount` indexes the six-entry `ramSizes` with `Creative`'s id 6. The `brain.conf` one is also answered where it bites (`smoke-test.sh:403-408` builds the config from ocelot-brain's complete `application.conf`), but the other two are avoided without being named: harness signals carry no arguments and `ocljit.ramtier` offers no creative tier (`OcljSmoke.scala:3752-3761`). Nothing under `test/native` mentions either, so the harness README note that research/ocelot-brain.md:52 asked for is still missing. **Corrected 2026-10-03:** the signal loss is upstream OpenComputers', not ocelot-brain's (GTNH `Machine.scala:865-879` in the 1.12.55 sources has the same fall-through that ocelot-brain mirrors at `:800-814`), so it bites in game too, and it covers more than `Integer`: `convertArg` keeps `Byte` and `Short` and turns `Character` into `Integer` (`:288-291`), and none of them has a save case. Only the creative-memory and `brain.conf` defects are ocelot-brain's own. **Status 2026-10-10:** Unchanged: no README under `test/`, and nothing under `test/native` names the signal loss or the creative-memory throw. `docs/research/ocelot-brain.md:52` still calls all three ocelot-brain defects, against the correction above; the harness note and that line are what remains. Filing the signal loss with GTNH's OpenComputers is outward-facing and needs the user.

**What a green harness run does NOT prove**, and this matters more than what it
does: it is not Minecraft (no energy, no `SaveHandler` deferral or world-save
ordering, no tile-entity lifecycle, no inventory/driver resolution — which is
precisely the one method whose body differs between the two `Architecture`
interfaces, so `recomputeMemory` is structurally unexercisable here), and it is
not GTNH. Deeper still: it cannot prove the two things the shape census says
actually kill real machines — PatchGuard-style `tostring(f)` fingerprinting and
unrebased `computer.uptime()` deadlines. Both are true of real Eris too, so the
harness will show us passing exactly where stock OpenComputers also fails,
which is comforting and irrelevant. **Corrected 2026-10-03:** the uptime half is withdrawn. OC persists and restores `uptime` (GTNH `Machine.scala:799`, `:886`), so a deadline survives a save, and what remains is an OS clock that mixes uptime with a wall-clock epoch ([research/os-shape-census.md](research/os-shape-census.md):388-418). Only `tostring(f)` fingerprinting remains unprovable here, and it is a documentation item (the player-facing docs row). The census's top-ranked silent corruptions, #1 and #3, were measured in the harness on both arms (M4.5's kernel half).

## v1 — Working architecture

The goal: OpenOS boots and runs on the LuaJIT architecture, with the JIT compiler deliberately left off (CCLuaJIT parity: ~1.5–3× vs PUC 5.3, zero trace-safety risk while the plumbing matures). **Superseded -- 2026-10-03:** the interpreter-only stage never happened, which is why "interpreter-only" has left this section's title. The JIT has been on since 2026-09-01: the binding's first OpenOS boot was a JIT-off arm, and it booted with the JIT on the same day, once a CHECKHOOK build dissolved the bogomips hang ([research/jnlua-binding.md](research/jnlua-binding.md)); trace safety is carried by the watchdog (the deadline-hook row below, and v2's adopt-the-watchdog row).

- [x] **HOW THIS SHIPS — RESOLVED, and it is our own `Architecture`, not native substitution** ([research/shipping-model.md](research/shipping-model.md)). Everything proven so far was proven by SUBSTITUTING our native under OpenComputers' own PUC-Lua architecture (`debug.forceNativeLibPathFirst`, which is real in shipping GTNH OC at `LuaStateFactory.scala:200-210`, not just in the harness). **That can never be a product, for a structural reason**: OC reads its config and `System.load`s its native in the same `preInit` (`Proxy.scala:32` then `:74`, with `init()` in each `LuaStateFactory` object's constructor), and we declare `required-after:OpenComputers`, so FML sorts us after it — there is no moment at which our code could set the key. It is also a `debug.*` key a player would hand-edit, has no IMC hook (OC's `IMC.java` offers ten, none for natives), and is destructive rather than additive: our DLL would have to be *named* `libjnlua52-windows-x86_64.dll` and every computer in the world would switch at once with no way back. **Keep it as the benchmark/CI harness — that is what it is for.** The product is `api.Machine.add`, which OC's own source calls out for exactly this: *"This allows providing architectures without implementing a custom CPU item."* Players sneak+right-click a CPU to cycle architectures (`CPULike.scala:28-48`); the choice is stored on the ItemStack and **degrades gracefully if our mod is removed** (`DriverCPU.scala:52-57`); and because OC registers in preInit and we register in init, we can never become the silent default. `OCLuaJIT.java` already registers correctly and `LuaJITArchitecture` already has the `@Architecture.Name` and the required `Machine` constructor. **THREE THINGS THIS ALSO DE-RISKED, each previously an assumption**: GTNH 1.12.58's `machine.lua` differs from the harness's by ONE line in 1548 (`realTime = computer.realTime`); `patch-machine-lua.lua` applies cleanly to the real GTNH kernel (4 sites, exit 0, and it refuses rather than guessing when an anchor misses); and `LuaStateFactory` is a line-for-line port between the two. **ocelot-brain is representative.** Cost of the build-out: of the ten `Architecture` methods, 4 are trivial, 1 small, `initialize` is 20 lines fronting ~823 lines of `ArchitectureAPI` glue, and 4 are hard — `runThreaded` (~107 lines, the yield-classification state machine, and it must self-enforce `Settings.timeout`), `runSynchronized` (a strict stack-shape protocol whose violation crashes the computer), and `save`/`load` (the only parts reaching outside the API, for `SaveHandler` and the internal `Machine.state`). **It is a diff-port, not a from-scratch write**: ocelot-brain's `NativeLuaArchitecture` is ~85% identical to OC's, with OC's original lines left commented above each replacement at all four persistence sites. **Superseded -- 2026-10-03:** no port was written. `LuaJITArchitecture` subclasses OC's `NativeLuaArchitecture` (the v0.5 split row) and overrides only `factory()`, `initialize()` (the kernel swap, next row) and `save()`/`load()` (the `_stack` row); `runThreaded` and `runSynchronized` are inherited (the component-call row). And "everything proven so far was proven by substituting our native" stopped being true that night: AxisOS booted on our architecture on 2026-09-15 (the v0.5 split row), and the smoke suite's persistence milestones moved onto it on 2026-09-16 (the v0.5 run-on-our-architecture row).
- [x] **Watchdog kernel delivery — SOLVED 2026-09-16, by replacing what initialize() loaded rather than how it loads.** `NativeLuaArchitecture.initialize()` loads the kernel from a fixed path in **OpenComputers'** resource domain, and the harness only got its patched kernel in by shadowing that path on a classpath it controls — which a mod cannot do, since FML orders mod jars for us and OC's jar holds that exact resource. Two routes were rejected before the third: **overriding `initialize()` outright is impossible**, because four of its sixteen lines are `apis.foreach(_.initialize())` and `apis` is private with no accessor in either host; and **intercepting `debug.sethook` in the native**, which would need no kernel of ours at all, founders on `machine.lua:47` calling `debug.sethook(coroutine.running(), checkDeadline, "", 1)` — the same function as the three deadline arms with a different count — so telling them apart from C is a heuristic that mis-fires silently where `patch-machine-lua.lua` refuses loudly. What works: let `super.initialize()` do all of its work, then drop the kernel thread it left on the stack, load ours from **our own** resource domain (`/assets/ocluajit/lua/machine.lua`), and make a thread of it again. Six lines. Proven in the harness with the patched kernel present ONLY at our path and nothing at OpenComputers': `_OCLJ_KERNEL=watchdog`, 147 traces against ~2500 under the standing hook, sandbox loop 0.0047 s against 0.47 s, 32 checks 0 failures. The mod-side copy is the same sequence through OC's public `lua()` accessor (ocelot-brain hides it behind `private[machine]`, so the harness reaches it reflectively) and is compiled, not run. (**2026-10-03:** it has run in game since 2026-09-16: the first in-game run row, the `_ENV` row's in-game verification, and T4.)
- [x] **The deadline watchdog MISSED ENTIRELY in 2 of ~6 runs under host load** (`fires=0`: the timer-queue callback was never delivered inside a 15 s window, so a runaway sandbox loop would not have been interrupted at all). A watchdog that is usually on time is not a watchdog; this is a reliability property, not a latency one, and it needs its own measurement before any JIT work leans on it. **Investigated in the harness 2026-09-19 — verdict INCONCLUSIVE, and the investigation found a different, real defect; see the `fires=0` contention row and the caught-timeout kernel-panic row below; the open work is the watchdog-delivery-under-load row.**
- [x] **`d-autorun-counter-live` fixed 2026-09-16 — and it was the boot wait, not the milestone.** It failed in every stock-KERNEL arm (`additive+stock`, `luajit+stock`) with `nonce=<missing>` and `counter -1`. The cause was one level up: the boot loop waits for the shell prompt AND the autorun's first line, but capped at a flat 600 ticks, and OpenComputers' standing count hook makes boot roughly a hundred times slower — so the loop left on its timeout with the shell up and the autorun line not yet printed, `c-openos-shell` passed (it asserts only the prompt), and `d` failed describing the symptom. Both waits now follow the roadmap's own rule, *wait until a condition with a timeout*: the cap is sized for the slowest legitimate arm (it only binds when something is wrong, so it costs the fast arms nothing), `d` waits until the counter ADVANCES rather than stepping a fixed 60 ticks, and `c` now says which way its loop left. Measured after: `counter 9 -> 29 in 10 of 2000 ticks` — the counter had been advancing all along.
- [x] **Decide what a stock-KERNEL arm should ASSERT, now that one can finish.** Fixing the boot wait let `additive+stock` run to completion for the first time, and it exposed `p0-compute-checksum: FAIL -- mandelbrot CHECK=too_long_without_yielding`. That is not a defect: under the standing hook our VM genuinely cannot compute the reference workload inside OC's deadline, which is precisely the "before" picture hook-vs-jit.md exists to show. But it means a documented control arm still reports SMOKE FAIL — now for the true reason rather than a bogus one. The harness already has the right pattern for this (`k5-baseline-has-no-watchdog`, `m1-baseline-has-no-mcode` assert the ABSENCE that defines a baseline), so the fix is to invert `p0` for stock-kernel arms rather than to relax it. Left undone deliberately: turning a real failure into an expected pass is a measurement-semantics decision, not a cleanup. **Superseded -- 2026-10-03:** decided and landed 2026-09-16 (commit `a2d5b34`) as accept-either, not an inversion. On a stock-kernel-on-our-native arm, `p0` passes on the reference checksum or on exactly `too_long_without_yielding`, and any other value still fails, so the fast-wrong-answer defence stands (`OcljSmoke.scala:4685-4324`); unlike `k5`/`m1`, it does not assert the baseline's defining absence, so the arm also passes if mandelbrot finishes. The `additive+stock` arm this row describes no longer runs under the default bundled-roots save (OC's own kernel fails its sync-call check after one tick), so the branch is reachable only on the dropin native ([research/hook-vs-jit.md](research/hook-vs-jit.md):428-435).
- [x] **Licensing and attribution — both files are wrong and the jar ships one of them.** The root `LICENSE` still reads `Copyright (c) 2021 Johann Bernhardt`, the GTNH ExampleMod template author; `src/main/resources/LICENSE`, which goes INSIDE the jar, is an unfilled template (`Copyright (c) [year] [fullname]`). Separately, the shipped artifacts now bundle third-party code with no attribution: LuaJIT (MIT, Mike Pall), OC-JNLua (MIT), and `serializer/eris_lj.c`, a derivative of Eris. Needs the project's own copyright line and a NOTICE/THIRD-PARTY file covering what the jar actually contains. Blocking for any public release. **Status 2026-10-03:** unchanged (`LICENSE:3` still names Johann Bernhardt, the jar's `LICENSE` is the unfilled template, and there is no NOTICE file), and the list above is short of what the jar contains. The jar also ships OpenComputers' `machine.lua`, patched, at `assets/ocluajit/lua/machine.lua`, with no notice (OpenComputers is MIT, Florian Nücke). LuaJIT's `COPYRIGHT` also carries a PUC-Rio notice that a NOTICE has to reproduce, and dlmalloc's public-domain statement; the simplest course is to ship LuaJIT's `COPYRIGHT` whole. `LuaStateLuaJIT` is generated from OC-JNLua's sources, so OC-JNLua's notice covers it too. README.md's acknowledgements now name the bundled projects; that is not the NOTICE this row asks for. **Status 2026-10-10: done.** The holder is Arets Paeglis (the user's decision), MIT kept: `LICENSE` and the jar's `src/main/resources/LICENSE` now read `Copyright (c) 2026 Arets Paeglis`. `NOTICE`, at the root and in `src/main/resources/` (the jar's root: `processResources` copies that directory, and GTNH's convention plugin adds nothing to it), names each bundled component, where it is used, and reproduces its license text verbatim: LuaJIT's `COPYRIGHT` whole (with its PUC-Rio and dlmalloc notices); Lua 5.2.4's and 5.3.6's notices, for the `lauxlib.c` and `lbitlib.c` functions `lj52shim.c` adapts (`luaL_getsubtable`, `luaL_tolstring` with 5.3's `__tostring` check, `luaL_requiref` from 5.3, `bit32`'s field checks and extract/replace); OC-JNLua's licence (JNLua's, with OpenComputers' preface; `LuaStateLuaJIT` is generated from its sources); Eris's notice (`eris_lj.c` follows its design and structure, with no copied lines); OpenComputers' MIT code licence (the patched `machine.lua`, and the save/load and `openLibs` code `LuaJITArchitecture` and `LuaJITStateFactory` reproduce); the MinGW-w64 runtime's (the Windows DLL statically links runtime objects, gdtoa among them, whose notice must travel with the binary); a note on libgcc, which both natives link statically and whose GCC Runtime Library Exception asks for no notice; and, for this repository only, the GTNH ExampleMod template's MIT notice (build scaffolding; no template code ships) and ocelot-brain's (`test/native/OcljArch.scala` reproduces its `NativeLuaArchitecture` save and load). Every reproduced text was checked byte for byte against its source. README.md points at both files and has a License section. The unused root `LICENSE-template` is left as it was. Not done, and moved 2026-10-10 to the jar staleness gate row: a build check that the root and resource copies of `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` stay identical.
- [x] **Native packaging — DONE 2026-09-16.** The VM now reaches the player: `build.gradle.kts` stages `build/native/dist/libjnluajit52-*` into the jar at `assets/opencomputers/lib/`, which is where OpenComputers' own `LuaStateFactory` resolves a classpath library (we subclass that factory rather than write a loader). `build-native.sh` collects every ADDITIVE artifact into `dist/` — never the dropin — so building on several platforms accumulates and one jar carries them all. Verified three ways: native present → it is in the jar; **no** native → `./gradlew build` still green with a clear log line, which is what CI needs since the runner cannot build a native; and `-Pocljit.nativesDir` pointed at the dropin directory → **refused**. That last guard took three attempts to make fireable — a relative `File()` resolved against the daemon's CWD, then a `Copy` with no matching sources was skipped `NO-SOURCE` taking its `doFirst` with it — so it lives in its own always-running task now. Nothing is checked in; binaries stay out of git.
- [x] **Package the patched kernel into the jar — DONE 2026-09-16.** `native/kernel/build-kernel.sh` extracts `assets/opencomputers/lua/machine.lua` from the OpenComputers jar **pinned in dependencies.gradle** (read from that file, not "whatever jar is lying around"), runs `patch-machine-lua.lua` over it, and writes `build/native/kernel/machine.lua`; `build.gradle.kts` stages that into the jar at `assets/ocluajit/lua/`, alongside the native. Verified: **GTNH's** kernel is the one patched — it carries `realTime = computer.realTime`, ocelot-brain's does not — and the jar holds both assets (47607 B kernel, 787740 B native). With neither present `./gradlew build` is still green and says what is missing and what that costs, which is what CI needs since the runner has neither a C toolchain nor a Lua interpreter. The step asserts its OUTPUT rather than the patcher's exit status: the `_OCLJ_KERNEL` marker present, `_OCLJ_WATCHDOG` read, exactly 3 `watchdog.arm` sites and exactly 3 surviving `debug.sethook` calls (2 since kernel site 12, 2026-09-21, which deleted `checkDeadline`'s re-arm; `build-kernel.sh` now asserts the bogomips arm and its clear) — a kernel that silently lost one arm site would leave that third path on OpenComputers' standing hook, and nothing else would notice. Also fixed while here: the patcher's success line reported `#src` AFTER substitution, so it printed `47162 -> 47607` for a 46483-byte input; it now reports the original.
- [x] **Cross-platform natives: the BUILD is portable as of 2026-09-16; the SHIM is not.** (**2026-10-03:** it became portable to x86-64 Linux the same day, the next row. macOS still needs its own backend (the macOS row), and linux-aarch64 needs the watchdog's x86-TSO ordering reviewed (the platform-matrix row).) `native/build-native.sh` now detects the host and derives everything from it — the artifact name (`libjnluajit52-linux-x86_64.so`), the JNI `jni_md.h` subdirectory, the linker flags (`--enable-stdcall-fixup` is PE-only and an error on ELF), and **`-fPIC` spliced into the LUAJIT build**, without which a `BUILDMODE=static` archive will not link into a shared object on x86-64 ELF. The LuaJIT and object trees are per-platform too (`luajit-$PLATFORM`, `obj-$PLATFORM`), because they were one shared directory and the Windows build leaves the `luajit.exe` that both the harness and `build-kernel.sh` run. Proven on WSL Ubuntu 22.04: LuaJIT builds clean with PIC in 21 s, `host = linux-x86_64 (lib*.so, jni_md in linux/)`, and the Windows build is unchanged (89 exports, `LuaStateLuaJIT_*`=87, `LuaState_*`=0).
  - The **postflight** was the careful part and is now one reader with two backends and a single set of assertions, so neither platform can be waved through on weaker checks than the other. It had to branch: `nm -D` on a PE DLL prints "no symbols" and **exits 0**, so a naive port would have counted zero `LuaState_*` and called an additive build clean. The reader is checked for emptiness first, for the same reason. It earned its keep immediately — it caught the first version of itself matching objdump's `RVA` column header as a 90th export.
- [x] **A LINUX watchdog backend — DONE 2026-09-16, and the Linux native builds and passes its regression suite.** Not "POSIX": `pthread_condattr_setclock` is absent on macOS, so a Darwin build would have to put the deadline condvar on `CLOCK_REALTIME` and inherit an NTP-step hazard on a live deadline. The `#error` narrowed rather than vanished, and macOS gets its own backend when there is a machine to build and test one on. **Shape:** one parked thread per `lj52_mem` in `pthread_cond_timedwait` on a `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` condvar; the mutex that thread holds across `lj52_wd_inject` **is** the blocking cancel, and it is strictly stronger than `DeleteTimerQueueTimer` — afterwards no injection can even START, because only the Lua thread sets `wd_due`. Chosen over `timer_create` (glibc spawns a thread per expiry; 20/s per firing machine), `SIGEV_SIGNAL` (a signal handler inside a JVM that owns SIGSEGV, and `lj_dispatch_update` is not async-signal-safe) and `timerfd`+epoll (wins at scale but needs a process-global registry keyed by machine, and still needs the per-machine mutex). Costs no syscall per arm or disarm in the steady state, which matters because both run on every sandbox coroutine resume. **Measured: `wd_test` 32/32 on Linux and 32/32 on Windows**, including W2 (a 50 ms deadline interrupts a compiled `while true do end` at 55.1 ms), W6b (4M iterations in 12.8 ms with 1 trace, against ~900 ms and ~200 traces under a standing hook), W9c (the re-fire restores a hook cleared behind the watchdog's back) and W13a (1 s of hook-per-instruction with ~20 re-fires and no `HOOK_ACTIVE` wedge — the failure the atomic-OR discipline exists to make impossible).
- [x] **The `fires=0` finding — CPU contention is NOT its cause (measured 2026-09-16), and the 2026-09-19 harness matrix leaves it INCONCLUSIVE.** The 09-16 result first: Both backends were run under the same reproduction attempt: 48 busy loops on 16 cores, 3x oversubscription, `wd_test` unchanged. **Linux fired 6 of 6** (W2 at 55.9-73.7 ms against a 50 ms deadline) and **Win32 fired 5 of 5** (55.0-71.6 ms). Since the backend that HAS the fault passes this test, the test does not reproduce the fault, and the Linux result is **not** evidence of immunity — the same shape as the N4 "regression" that evaporated once PUC was measured on the same workload. Worst lateness under load was ~24 ms on either backend, against ~5 ms quiet.
  - **Where to look instead:** the original `fires=0` was seen in the ocelot-brain HARNESS, not in standalone `wd_test` — a JVM with 100+ threads, its own GC pauses and OC's per-machine executors. Reproduce it there, with the watchdog stats read out per run, before attributing it to anything.
  - The design's claim that delivery being DERIVED rather than registered makes a lost wake inexpressible remains plausible and unproven. It cannot be proven by a test the old backend also passes.
  - **2026-09-19, the harness matrix (25 serialised runs, four load arms, a per-run `WDTIMELINE` readout that separates armed-and-not-delivered from never-armed, and two adversarial readers).** No run that produced a reading printed `fires=0`: 6/6 unloaded and 6/6 under a mild JVM load (two JVMs x 6 threads), the probe's spin 4.7–5.3 s in both (the 5 s deadline plus sampling), i.e. **on time at ~1.1x**. But no steady load that slowed the harness by ~2x or more let a run COMPLETE: under 16 spin loops plus JVM threads OpenOS boot died with `kernel panic` 9/9; under 16 JVM threads 3/4 hit the harness's tick-counted boot caps before the probe was planted and 1/4 **fired on time and then the kernel died 0.4 s later**. So the 12 heavily loaded runs are censored, not zeros, and 0/6 at 1.1x has P=0.09 against a true rate of 1/3 — no refutation. Two things the readers found about the ORIGINAL number: the 09-03 harness (commit `410b3ee`) read the stats with an UNLOCKED `evalStr` after waiting at most 6 s for the machine to go idle, so its `fires=0` could have been read while the probe was still spinning (the k1 result of those runs was never checked; the logs do not survive) — today's `evalStrLocked` read cannot do that; and a steady generator slows every resume (mandelbrot 3.82 s vs 0.16 s = 24x executor starvation) where the original batch's bursty builds evidently did not, since it got past boot every time. **What the matrix DID establish is the kernel-panic route in the next row, which explains every death it saw.** To reopen `fires=0` honestly: a burstier load, uptime-based rather than tick-based harness caps, and the (a)-direction fixture (a scratch native with the fire emptied) so the readout's armed-and-not-delivered branch is shown, not asserted. The `WDTIMELINE` readout stays in the harness as a print-only diagnostic (`k1_ms`, `exec_run_ms`, `quiesce_ms`, `read_ms`, the five shim counters). **Tracked since 2026-10-03** in the watchdog-delivery-under-load row.
- [x] **A CAUGHT TIMEOUT THAT OUTLIVES ITS GRACE WAS A KERNEL PANIC ON OUR ARCHITECTURE AND A CLEAN CRASH ON STOCK — found by the 2026-09-19 load matrix, closed 2026-09-21 by kernel site 12.** OC's `checkDeadline` re-arms itself with `debug.sethook(coroutine.running(), checkDeadline, "", 1)` on its first raise (count=1 from then on, so a program that catches `too long without yielding` and keeps running is killed for real at `deadline + 0.5`). On PUC that hook is per-thread; on LuaJIT hook state lives in the `global_State`, so the re-arm installed an UNFILTERED count=1 hook on every thread — replacing the shim's thread-filtered C hook — and the patcher had left it in place on the reasoning that `disarm()` clears it. It does, but the kernel's own instructions between `coroutine.resume` returning and `watchdog.disarm(wd)` are hooked too: once the grace expires in the sandbox, the uncaught second raise returns to the kernel thread, whose next instruction raises inside `main`, `pcall(main)` catches that, and the chunk's tail call `return pcallTimeoutCheck(pcall(main))` runs outside any pcall — its first instruction raises again and escapes the kernel coroutine. OC reports `kernel panic: this is a bug, check your log file and report it` with `Kernel crashed. This is a bug!` in the log. **Deterministic, no load needed, player-reachable:** `pcall(function() while true do end end)` followed by a 1 s busy-wait on `computer.uptime()`. New harness mode `OCLJ_PROBE=grace` (boot, plant only that program, one milestone `k6-grace-expiry-crashes-cleanly`: machine stopped, `lastError` contains `too long without yielding`, not `kernel panic`, AND the death came no earlier than the 5 s timeout measured from the spin row, so a first raise that escaped the program's `pcall` cannot pass it vacuously; `OCLJ_GRACE_MIN_MS=99999` shows the clause failing). **Fail-first:** on the 11-site kernel `lastError='kernel panic: ...'`, the WARN captured (`LuaRuntimeException: thread: 0x33ad2cd0`), death 5411 ms after the spin began = timeout + ~0.4 s, the same 0.4 s the load matrix saw; on `OCLJ_NATIVE=stock` (PUC 5.2 + OC's own kernel) `lastError='too long without yielding'`, no WARN, 5415 ms. **Fix:** patcher site 12 deletes that one line inside `checkDeadline` (the grace arithmetic and `hitDeadline` are untouched); the shim's hook is already count=1, thread-filtered and re-fired every 50 ms from the first fire until `disarm` (`wd_test` W9c/W13a), so the Lua re-arm added nothing on our native and only removed the filter. `build-kernel.sh` asserts `surviving debug.sethook=2` (the bogomips arm and clear) by content, refusing with a message naming site 12 — shown failing three ways (new postflight on the 11-site patcher: `found 3`; the patcher's own count assert without site 12: `3 debug.sethook( calls remain`; a neutered scratch copy). Kernel 51952 B, md5 `089dcbde`, byte-identical across two builds. **In-game confirmed 2026-09-22** (T4 in [in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md): the blue `too long without yielding` screen, a normal reboot after, no `Kernel crashed` in the log). **Gates on the 12-site kernel:** `k6` PASS 4/4 (`lastError='too long without yielding'`, no WARN, death 5402–5421 ms after the spin began; the timing clause shown failing with `OCLJ_GRACE_MIN_MS=99999`); full suite 46/0 with k1/k4/k5 PASS and the shim's filter now visibly doing the work (`stats fires/refires/filtered = 1/0/5`: five fires on the kernel's own instructions skipped); `wd_test` 32/0. Natives untouched. **Parity after the fix** (source-level, reviewed): a program that catches the timeout and yields inside the grace lives on both stacks; one that spins past it dies cleanly on both; the plain uncaught overrun is unchanged. Two residuals recorded: (1) **a leaked arm entry is a pre-existing shim hole** — if an error is raised between the sandbox wrapper's raw `coroutine.resume` returning and its `disarm(wd)` (`machine.lua:883-886`; only `LUA_ERRMEM` at that one allocation, or a stack-overflow inside `callhook`, can do it), the entry stays with `wd_for = child` and the filter skips the parent until it yields to its own parent; a `while true do end` there wedged the executor before and after this fix, and a `while true do pcall(f) end` there used to die by the panic route and now wedges too. Contrived (memory exhaustion timed at one allocation, then never yielding), but the filter should not skip a parent forever. **Closed 2026-09-22 in the shim: each arm entry carries a skip budget (`wd_skip[]`, `LJ52_WD_SKIPMAX`); once a parent has been skipped that many times for one live entry the hook stops filtering it and `checkDeadline` runs on the parent as PUC's per-thread hook would, so the leak degrades to the deadline killing the program instead of a hang. Two `wd_test` cases, failing first on the old shim (the parent's callback never called), `wd_test` 35/0.** (2) **JNLua loses every kernel-panic message**: `lua_1resume` throws from `L` without moving the error object off `T`, so the exception was built from `tostring(L, 1)` = the kernel thread object (`thread: 0x...`). **Closed 2026-09-22: `native/jnlua/patch-resume-error.sh` applies one line (`lua_xmove(T, L, 1)` before `throw`) to the build copy of OC-JNLua's `jnlua.c` for both variants, anchored on the unique context inside `lua_1resume` and asserted exactly once; the checkout stays byte-identical to upstream. Gate `jn-1-resume-error-carries-message` (a coroutine erroring with a string and one with a `__tostring` table, resumed through jnlua on the raw state): PASS on the new native (`LuaRuntimeException: jn1-string-error` / `jn1-tostring-error`), FAIL on the pre-bump DLL (`thread: 0x36566260`). Recorded, not asserted: when the error object's own `__tostring` raises, jnlua's `throw` falls back to `java.lang.Error("error throwing Lua exception")` — the machine shows `Error.InternalError`; that is jnlua's pre-existing fallback, which the old 11-site kernel reaches (its still-armed global hook raises inside the sentinel's `__tostring`) and the shipped 12-site kernel cannot, because nothing escapes `pcall(main)` there. Stock OC's own kernel-panic logs very likely carry the same `thread: 0x` blind spot.** The stale `lj52shim.c` comments were rewritten in the same change.
- [x] **THE FIRST IN-GAME RUN — 2026-09-16, and the adapter works.** Installed into a real ~200-mod GTNH-based instance on **OpenComputers 1.12.61-GTNH** (we pin 1.12.58; the skew is inert — `machine.lua` is byte-identical between them and all three classes we subclass have identical opcode streams, `ldc`/`ldc_w` width aside). Forge loaded the mod, `Found a compatible native library: 'OpenComputersMod-1.12.61-GTNH-jit52-libjnluajit52-windows-x86_64.dll'` and `Registered the LuaJIT architecture with OpenComputers.` one millisecond apart — that first line only logs after `System.load` AND a real throwaway state construct+close, so the native ran inside a Forge JVM. Architecture selectable on the CPU (`Lua 5.4 -> Lua 5.2 -> LuaJ -> LuaJIT`, our `Machine.add` appended after OC's preInit four, which is also why removing the jar falls back to **Lua 5.3**). OpenOS 1.8.9 installed and booted at 16384k RAM at the pack's stock `ramScaleFor64Bit=1.8` — so the 1-in-6 boot failure recorded in the RAM-scale row did NOT reproduce, though on far more RAM than that measurement used, and the emergency collector has landed since; treat that item as untested rather than fixed. Identity confirmed three ways from inside the sandbox: `computer.getArchitecture()` = `LuaJIT`, `string.dump` header = `LJ`, `tostring(rawget)` = `function: builtin#12`.
  - **THE JIT WORKS IN MINECRAFT, measured.** 4M iterations of `x = x + i % 7` in **0.0065 s cpu** and 20M in **0.0296 s** — 615 and 676 M iter/s, near-linear (5x work, 4.55x time, slightly sublinear as trace warmup amortises). The same shape of loop measured **0.473 s** under OC's standing hook, so this is ~70x past the thrashing case. Both checksums verified exact (11999998, 60000003). Compute half only: the bimodal finding stands, and a component-bound test in-game will be flat by design.
  - Measurement note: `computer.uptime()` is tick-derived, so anything under 50 ms reads 0 — the first run's `real=0.0000` was the resolution floor, not a failure. `os.clock()` is in the sandbox and reports machine CPU time; on the 20M row `real=0.0500` against `cpu=0.0296` shows it correctly excluding unscheduled ticks.
- [x] **THE `_ENV` GAP — found in-game, and the compatibility audit was wrong.** `lua` died with `lua_shell.lua:14: attempt to index global '_ENV' (a nil value)`. LuaJIT is API/ABI-locked to 5.1 and cannot implement `_ENV`; `feasibility.md:68` and `report-compat-and-perf.md:28,93` asserted OpenOS never reads it. **It reads it at eleven sites across five files.** Both documents are now corrected in place rather than quietly edited.
  - **The silent half was worse than the crash.** An unbound global reads as nil, so `boot/01_process.lua:68`'s `env = _ENV` set the init process's env to nil, `lib/process.lua:32` propagated it down the whole tree, and `machine.lua`'s own `env or sandbox` default then handed **every program the raw sandbox**. Per-process environments were collapsed machine-wide since first boot, with nothing raised anywhere.
  - **The obvious fix is wrong and was rejected on evidence.** `sandbox._ENV = sandbox` alone resolves everywhere (every OpenOS env chains to `_G`) while answering with the sandbox instead of the chunk's own environment — silently breaking `.install` floppies' documented `_ENV.install` contract, letting program globals escape the shell's shadow table into `_G`, and making the `lua` REPL's require cache inject every module typed into the sandbox globals permanently. Established with an executable replica of `sandbox.load` + OpenOS's `intercept_load` + `shell.lua:21` + `process.lua:52`, run on our own `luajit.exe`; 9 of the 11 sites have a non-sandbox `_ENV`.
  - **Shipped fix:** two patch sites. Per-chunk `rawset(env, "_ENV", env)` in the sandbox `load` wrapper (raw accessors because OpenOS envs carry `__index`/`__newindex` that would otherwise hide the key or misdirect the write), plus `sandbox._ENV = sandbox` as the base case for kernel-loaded chunks such as the BIOS. `build-kernel.sh` asserts the per-chunk site **by name** — a bare `grep _ENV` would pass the wrong fix — and the check was proven to fail against a kernel carrying only the base case.
  - **VERIFIED IN-GAME 2026-09-16, and verified DISCRIMINATINGLY.** A loading `lua` shell proves nothing here — the wrong one-site fix would also make `lua_shell.lua:14` succeed. So: at the REPL `_ENV == _G` is **false** and `_ENV.print ~= nil` is **true** (a distinct env that still chains), and a program setting a global leaves `rawget(_G, ...)` **nil** — the containment check the one-site fix cannot pass. Deployment note learned the hard way: `NativeLuaArchitecture.load` opens with `lua.setTop(0)` and unpersists the saved kernel, so a machine RESTORED from a save keeps the kernel it was persisted with. The native is unchanged by this fix, so a stale blob is not rejected either — swapping the jar under a running machine looks exactly like the fix failing. Power the computer off before swapping.
  - **Not fixed, and unfixable:** `local _ENV = t` is an ordinary local under LuaJIT and `_ENV = t` does not rebind resolution. OpenOS does neither. Accepted divergences: `pairs(env)` now yields `_ENV`, and `rawget(_G, "_ENV")` is non-nil for us and nil on stock OC.
  - **Open:** the same audit cleared `utf8`, `bit32`, `string.pack` and `math.maxinteger` as "optional/guarded" by the method that just missed eleven `_ENV` references. Re-check them the same way — by grepping the OS, not the kernel. **2026-10-03:** never done; tracked since in the 5.2-class row, where `bit32` drops out of it (it is a real 5.2 `bit32` in C: the kernel row near the end of v1). **2026-10-17:** `string.pack` is no longer optional on our VM: our fork's string library has it (the 5.2-class row, and the string.pack row under v1).
- [x] **UNPERSIST RESTORES A `__persist` CLOSURE BEFORE THE UPVALUE IT CALLS — found in-game 2026-09-16; CLOSED 2026-09-17 by shell-fill ([shell-fill.md](shell-fill.md)).** The harness milestone below is green: `f7` PASS with its sequence number advanced 71→86, 38/0; and the negative control (OC's unpatched recipes on the new serializer) dies in ocelot-brain with `returned a table instead of filling its argument`. **Confirmed in-game the same day**: the exact shape that failed on 09-16 — `lua` open, an `io.open` handle held, save-and-quit, reload — resumes at the prompt with the marker intact and `f:read(20)` continuing at byte 21, i.e. the host-side file position survived; the log has no state-load error. Original finding follows. First in-game persistence test: save-and-quit with the `lua` REPL open, reload, and the machine refuses with `machine:1162: attempt to call upvalue 'wrapSingleUserdata' (a nil value)` from `PersistenceAPI.unpersist:164`. **Mechanism:** `u_table`'s TABLE_SPECIAL arm runs the restored `__persist` closure the instant its record is read (`eris_lj.c:1792`), while `u_thread` writes the restored thread's slots in ASCENDING order (`eris_lj.c:1896`) — so a closure holding an open upvalue over a not-yet-written slot reads nil. `machine.lua:753` declares `sandbox`, `:1075` declares `wrapSingleUserdata`, and the chunk ends in `pcall(main)` (not a tail call), so the frame stays live and both are OPEN upvalues; anything reachable from the sandbox subtree is rebuilt before slot 1075 is filled. `perms` cannot rescue it: `PersistenceAPI.initialize()` flattens `_G` **before** `machine.lua` loads (`NativeLuaArchitecture.scala:319` then `:321`), so every kernel local is serialized by value. The comment at `eris_lj.c:176-180` states the hazard word for word and nothing acts on it.
  - **NOT the `_ENV` change.** Reproduced identically on a chunk containing **zero** occurrences of `_ENV`; an 8-cell differential across both kernels is identical in stage and error text in every cell, differing only by the 12 bytes of the two self-referential keys. The rig was shown able to PASS — moving only where the proxy is reachable from walks the failure up the upvalue chain (`sandbox` → nil `wrapSingleUserdata`; a holder above the 1075 group → nil `wrappedUserdata` and `pairs(nil)`; above the whole chain → passes), which is exactly what ascending-slot restore predicts.
  - **What put a userdata in the blob: one leaked file handle, from the REPL's own first line.** `bin/lua.lua:10` is `io.lines(filename, "*a")()`; `io.lines` closes only on the nil/EOF branch and `"*a"` returns the whole body on the first read, so it never closes, and `boot/01_process.lua:81-88` pins every handle into `process.data.handles`. `FileSystem.HandleValue` is the ONE `api.machine.Value` an ordinary OpenOS session can reach. A bare idle `sh` holds none.
  - **ANSWERED 2026-09-16: the trigger IS the userdata path, and PERSISTENCE OTHERWISE WORKS IN-GAME.** Saved from a bare `sh` prompt with no handle open, exited to the main menu, reloaded: **the machine resumed.** A live OpenOS shell — a suspended coroutine stack and its whole environment — round-tripped through eris to disk and back inside Minecraft. So the defect is NOT generic to `__persist`-produced closures: `wrappedUserdataMeta`'s own metamethod firing with zero userdata held is fine, and only an actual wrapped-userdata proxy reachable from a low slot trips the ordering. That also means a `lua` REPL open at save time is currently the one known way to break a reload, via the leaked handle at `bin/lua.lua:10`.
  - **Where the fix goes, and why it is not a one-liner.** The hook exists: `I->rthreads` already tracks in-flight thread restores, and `elj_live_top` (`eris_lj.c:1638-1644`) already consults it to bound open-upvalue slots — the TABLE_SPECIAL arm at `:1784-1797` simply does not ask. The obvious fix is to defer `lua_call` while `I->rthreads != NULL` and run the pending closures once the slot pass completes. The complication to design around is REFERENCE RESOLUTION: the arm reserves its ref BEFORE the call precisely because the table does not exist until the closure returns, so a deferred call leaves `REFTIDX[ref]` empty for any other record that references that table in the meantime. A defer-by-default/force-on-demand scheme degrades to today's failure only in the genuinely cyclic case, which is the case that has no answer anyway — but that needs deciding, not assuming.
  - **THE HARNESS GAP IS CLOSED, AND THE TEST FAILS AS IT MUST (2026-09-16).** `OcljSmoke.scala` had no `userdata` at all, so its persist milestones asserted the blob EXISTED and that scalar-upvalued closures survived, never what SHAPE was in it — the workload even opened handles (`:602`) but closed them, so eris never reached one. Not a structural limit: ocelot-brain produces userdata exactly as OC does (`FileSystem.scala:162` returns a `HandleValue`, `PersistenceAPI` is a line-for-line port). Now the workload holds one open handle as an upvalue of the heartbeat closure, and two milestones were added: **`f1b-blob-carries-userdata`** scans the blob for the literal class name `HandleValue` (the anti-vacuity gate — without it `f7` passes on any run where no proxy was reachable), and **`f7-restored-userdata-proxy-live`** checks the proxy still reads `userdata` and still reaches its `HandleValue` after restore. Against the shipping build: `f1b` PASS (blobBytes=161498), then the player's exact error, `machine:1162: attempt to call upvalue 'wrapSingleUserdata' (a nil value)`, then 4 failures of 38 checks. The four-cell falsification matrix: fails on luajit+watchdog, fails identically on luajit with the UNPATCHED stock kernel at `machine:1140` (a second, independent exoneration of the `_ENV` patch), passes 34/0 on stock PUC Lua, and the anti-vacuity gate fails on the no-handle control.
  - **`f7` NEEDED A SAMPLE SEQUENCE NUMBER, and the first run proved why.** A machine killed during unpersist leaves the PRE-SAVE screen painted, so a check reading only the row's shape passes on a corpse — it did, on two dead machines, before the counter existed. `OCLJUD` now carries a counter only a live probe can advance, gated the way `encoreSeq` already is, and `f7` demands a sample newer than the pre-save one. First run: `before=table/userdata/true/8/71 after=table/userdata/true/8/70` — the restored screen shows an OLDER sample, and the shape string is byte-identical, so the naive check would have reported PASS on the dead machine. `f3` shows the same backwards jump (707 → 697), so the stale repaint is general, not a quirk of this probe.
  - ~~The suite is now RED by design until the serializer is fixed.~~ **Green as of 2026-09-17** (shell-fill steps 0–5a). It remains a manual harness outside CI.
  - **`disablePersistence` is NOT a workaround and its documentation is wrong for this architecture.** The advertised auto-reboot exists only in `LuaJLuaArchitecture.load`; on the native path `unpersist` returns false but `load` has already run `lua.setTop(0)` and throws `Invalid kernel.` — the identical failure with a different string. The supported alternative, if we ever want it, is OC's own non-persistent shape: override `load` with `machine.stop(); machine.start()` and make `save` a no-op (`Machine.load` has a `state.top == Restarting` case for exactly that).
  - **Recovery is one click:** the machine is cleanly Stopped (`Machine.scala` catch → `close()`), nothing needs deleting, no disk data is lost, and the stale blob is not retried once one clean save has written `state = [Stopped]`. Sneak+right-click cold-boots it. Note `/tmp` is NOT wiped on this path (that lives in `tryClose()`), so it is not a pristine cold boot.
- [x] **TWO SERIALIZER BUGS FOUND WHILE DESIGNING THE UNPERSIST FIX, BOTH FIXED 2026-09-16 — neither is the unpersist defect, and both existed independently of it.**
  - **A USE-AFTER-FREE IN THE FOR-IN REPLAY WALK.** `elj_push_replay_state` visited hash nodes BY KEY including nil-valued ones, deliberately (`eris_lj.c:768-777`: a key whose value is nil now is still visited in case the program restores it before the cursor arrives). But LuaJIT marks a node's key ONLY when its value is non-nil (`lj_gc.c`, `gc_traverse_tab`), so after `t[obj] = nil` the node keeps the pointer while the sweep is free to collect the object behind it — and `copyTV` then resurrected freed memory. Reachable from ordinary Lua: `cache[obj] = nil` around a loop another coroutine is suspended in. **Measured: a control build with the guard reverted segfaults inside `eris.persist` 18 times in 20; with the guard, 0 in 20.** Fix: visit a nil-valued node only when its key is not collectible (`tvisgcv`), which dereferences nothing because the type tag lives in the node. **The cost is real and is written into the comment**: a GC-typed key whose value is nil at save time is now dropped from the replay list, so a loop will not visit it even if the program restores `t[key]` first. There is no safe alternative — telling "still referenced elsewhere" from "only this dead node points at it" requires reading the object's mark bit, which is the unsafe access.
  - **THE BLOB FINGERPRINT DID NOT COVER THE SERIALIZER.** `ERIS_LJ_COMMIT` is the LUAJIT tree's HEAD (`build-native.sh:401`), so every edit to `eris_lj.c` shipped under an unchanged fingerprint and a blob written by the old restore code loaded silently under the new one — precisely the silent misload the fingerprint exists to refuse. Both build paths now hash the serializer sources (`build-native.sh` and `serializer/Makefile`, which agreed on `68bfc3cc`) and feed it in as `ERIS_LJ_SERHASH`. **Consequence to expect: every blob written before this commit is now refused with "build fingerprint mismatch" and the computer comes back stopped.** That is the intended behaviour and is loud rather than silent, but it means a persisted machine will not resume across this change.
  - No regressions: for-in 19/19 cases exact across every pad, contract all-pass, M1 82/82, M2 55/55, M3 75/75, `wd_test` 32/0.
  - **Three instrument artifacts cost real time here and are worth the warning.** (a) `make` did not rebuild after a source restore because the `.c` and the `.exe` landed in the same second, so a "fixed" measurement of 20/20 crashes was actually the control — both binaries were byte-identical in size with the same timestamp. Force the build and keep distinct filenames when running a control. (b) `run-forin.sh` uses one binary for save, relay AND load, so rebuilding underneath a running suite produces a fingerprint mismatch that looks exactly like a regression. (c) `r1_deadkey` does not discriminate in a single run — the ghost node is layout-dependent and a rehash evicts it; only the 60-run bisect or `r6_crash` is a reliable signal.
- [x] **PERSISTENCE, REDESIGNED FROM REQUIREMENTS — 2026-09-17, decision: SHELL-FILL** ([research/persistence-clean-slate.md](research/persistence-clean-slate.md)). Twelve must-haves derived from OC's contract; three prior-art surveys; four designs from different premises; two judges scoring against the requirements. **All four designs independently dropped retry** and measured that nothing needs it. Both judges chose the same design: the reader creates the special's FINAL table at record time (so every reference already points at the real object — not a placeholder, so L4's poisons vanish rather than get worked around) and the recipe is called as `recipe(shell)` once after the whole graph exists, filling it in place. 94 diff lines against the converged design's ~330; one linear pass; wire unchanged; two refusals proven to fire. **fnuecke proposed exactly this in Eris issue #9 (2014) and never built it because he did not control the userdata recipe. We do.** Prototype `SHELL.exe` verified independently: the failing-first test is 6/6 red on the repo binary and 0/6 on the prototype. Grafts: D3's reachability walker as the fill-order oracle (closes the one content-dependency silent mode), D4's rig as `serializer/tests/userdata.lua` with its negative control. **Two must-haves the list missed:** the `_stack` blob is a second kernel universe on this codec (violates M2 under every design whenever a save lands mid-sync-call — routine under `cat`), scheduled before any dispose work; and disposing a duplicate `HandleValue` closes the surviving proxy's file, so duplicates are dropped, never disposed. **Status 2026-10-03:** The design is built and verified. It covers CREATE at `serializer/eris_lj.c:2004-2023`, the drain at `:2296` with the walker graft (`tests/shell-order.lua`) and the refusals at `:2284`/`:2289`, and the kernel recipes that fill in place (`build/native/kernel/machine.lua:1107`, `:1180-1187` → `wrapUserdataInto` `:1237`). It passes harness `f1b`/`f7` (`OcljSmoke.scala:5078`/`:5420`) with the stock-kernel negative control and in-game T1 (2026-09-17), and the `_stack` must-have is closed (L141, `tests/stack-universe.lua`, `stk-1..4`, T2). Still open: the D4 graft. `serializer/shell-fill/rig.lua` was never promoted to `serializer/tests/userdata.lua` (`stack-universe.lua` covers its index-2 kernel mirror but not `RIG_MODE=orig`), the `udinvoke` nil-guard of `docs/shell-fill.md` §5.4 is absent (`machine.lua:1151-1157`), and `make test` (`serializer/Makefile:43-49`) runs neither `tests/shell.lua` nor `tests/shell-order.lua`, nor `shell-fill/shell_stress.lua` (the `M5 — hardening` row). One more for the promotion, a comment defect only: `serializer/tests/stack-universe.lua`'s header cites the kernel about 24 lines early (`wrapUserdataInto` at `:1213` is now `:1237`); the kernel's chunk-local declarations have not moved relative to one another, so the mirror still holds. **Status 2026-10-10:** Done: built and verified at HEAD. `serializer/` is unchanged since 83aeabd (2026-09-22); the recipes above are in the kernel the installed jar ships (`build/native/kernel/machine.lua`, md5 `1d12c322`, verified 2026-10-10); `f1b`/`f7` pass in all 20 full-suite runs on our natives in `bench/runs/2026-10-05-{window,errmem,reserve}`; and `tests/shell.lua`, `tests/shell-order.lua` and `tests/stack-universe.lua` print `FAILS: 0` on `serializer/erislj_test.exe` (serializer hash `8c5a1168`, the source at HEAD; run 2026-10-10). Merged 2026-10-10 into the `M5 — hardening` row: the leftovers above (the D4 rig port, the `udinvoke` nil-guard, the three tests outside `make test`, `stack-universe.lua`'s kernel refs).
- [x] **THE SHIPPED NATIVE WAS STALE FOR A DAY, AND THE JAR BUILD COULD NOT TELL — found and gated 2026-09-17.** `build-native.sh` defaults to `OCLJ_VARIANT=dropin`, which writes the harness's `libjnlua52-*` into `libdir/`; the jar ships the ADDITIVE `libjnluajit52-*` from `dist/`, produced only by `OCLJ_VARIANT=additive`. Two native builds (after the UAF/fingerprint fix on 09-16 and after shell-fill on 09-17) ran the default, so `dist/` kept the 09-16 12:09 DLL (`711d754a`): **the UAF fix and the serializer fingerprint were committed and never shipped**, and a jar was assembled pairing the shell-fill kernel with a serializer that calls recipes with no argument — the negative control, by accident. Caught only because the jar's DLL md5 was checked against the build's. **Gate:** `verifyModAssets` now hashes `serializer/eris_lj.c + eris_lj.h` exactly as the build does and refuses any packaged native whose bytes do not contain that fragment (`ERIS_LJ_SERHASH` is embedded in the binary), naming the file and the expected hash; skipped when no native is present so CI is unaffected. Proven to fail against the stale DLL. Both platforms rebuilt additive on the shell-fill serializer: Windows `a3b64511`, Linux `d3768017` (WSL), hash `25471fb0`, 87 `LuaStateLuaJIT_*` / 0 `LuaState_*` each.
- [x] **SHELL-FILL STEP 4 — the walker, 2026-09-17: recipes now fill in dependency order, and the last silent mode is closed.** Before any recipe runs, the reader walks each recipe's closure (upvalues open and closed, env, table parts, metatables, thread slots) and records every other unfilled shell it reaches; permanents are leaves (`PERMSET`, the inverse of `UPERMS`); hash nodes with nil values are skipped (L5); the walk is a stamped worklist bounded by `ERIS_LJ_REACH_BUDGET`. Phase 2 fills a ready shell at a time, highest ordinal first; if none is ready it refuses naming both ordinals. **Discriminators** (`tests/shell-order.lua`, 8 cases): recipe A reads B's contents tolerating nil, shaped so A has the higher ordinal — on the step-3 binary `ok=true, A.y=nil` (a silent wrong restore, measured), with the walker `A.y=42`; C1/C2 cycles refused; P1 permanent-is-leaf; B1 a 2000-table chain. **Negative controls:** delete the open-upvalue edge and D2 alone goes silent; delete the env edge and C1/C2/B1 change instead — each edge load-bearing for exactly the cases its medium predicts. **Cost on the verbatim kernel block: 20 visits per proxy, linear.** C review: no raw-pointer use after a stack-growing call, no ghost-node read, no C recursion. Three minor findings fixed after review (nondeterministic blocked-on ordinal → lowest among the blocked recipe's deps, proven stable 5/5; honest refusal count; env-as-special is now ordered, not refused). **The fix itself had a bug the tests caught in seconds:** consuming the `lua_next` key before `continue` → `invalid key to 'next'` on all six walker cases. Final binary `1a9e8e17`: shell-order 0/8, shell 0/6, stress 0/9, M1 82, M2 56, M3 75, contract, for-in 19 exact. The serializer hash moved to `c945e096` and the jar guard refused both stale natives by name before they were rebuilt.
- [x] **THE `_stack` SECOND UNIVERSE — found by the requirements pass 2026-09-17, closed the same day in the two Architecture subclasses, no serializer change.** OC persists a running machine as two roots in two separate `eris.persist` calls: `_kernel` (the coroutine at index 1) and, mid-sync-call, `_stack` (the closure at index 2). The closure's four open upvalues into the kernel frame (`args`, `target`, `unwrapUserdata`, `wrapUserdata`; `machine.lua:1116-1124`) made `persist(2)` chase the owner through `elj_find_owner_any` — reached only when the owner is not already in the reference table, which it never was for a separate call — and pull the WHOLE kernel into `_stack`. **Measured** (`serializer/tests/stack-universe.lua` U1, kept as the documented defect): stack blob 11640 B vs kernel 11377, 4 host loads for 2 proxies, the restored closure aliasing a SECOND kernel, a sync result proxy `NOT-UNWRAPPED`. Stock Eris writes those upvalues by value, so stock's `_stack` is small but still a second universe in closed-copy form — "match stock" would have kept the silent mode. **The fix: one reference space.** `LuaJITArchitecture.java` and the harness adapter `OcljArch.scala` override `save`/`load` wholesale (super's cannot be wrapped; both persist unconditionally with no hook between), persist ONE table `{[1]=thread, [2]=closure|table}` as `_kernel`, and on load push `t[1]`/`t[2]` to the shape `runThreaded`/`runSynchronized` assert. Every side effect of `NativeLuaArchitecture.scala:349-437` reproduced in order and reviewed line by line: memory unlimit/relimit, `kernelMemory`, the api loop over the SAME `apis` array (one reflective read, resolved at construction so a renamed field is a machine that will not start; it reuses the original `PersistenceAPI`, whose private `persistKey` the kernel's recipes were keyed with — a fresh instance would silently miss every recipe), the GC-with-crash handler, `recomputeMemory`, and `removeTag("state")` on failure. U2 on the shipping serializer: 11650 B = kernel + **273**, one load per proxy, `upvalue-id/args-slot/registry` all identical to a never-saved machine. **Harness gate `stk-1..4`** saves WHILE the machine is in `SynchronizedCall` (a burst of direct `fs.open`s past the per-tick limit): override on, 42/0, `_stack=absent`; **override off, 42/3** — `_stack=164838 B, ratio 1.00`, 14 loads for 7 proxies, and the sharpest measurement of the harm: `close:bad_file_descriptor` on a handle from the second universe.
