A state that never hands over (the dropin, which backs OC's own class; an uncapped state; an old class on a new DLL) stays on the old path. In the JVM arm capped binarytrees reads 0.217–0.234 s against 0.385–0.399 s (uncapped 0.207–0.219); in the pinned harness machine, the class the only difference, 0.2500–0.2564 s against 0.4239–0.4247, strings2 0.84–0.88x, matmul 0.92–0.94x, the pure loops unmoved. `freeMemory` exact to the byte against LuaJIT's own count at every reading, mid-call included. Gates: `mem_test` 49/0 (twenty new checks, seen failing on the pre-change object and on nine sabotaged shims), harness 65/0 with eight new `acc-*` milestones (each seen failing under a sabotaged class), per-variant export counts (additive 89 `Java_*` / 91 PE names, dropin unchanged), a regenerate-and-diff gate for the class, and `verifyModAssets` refusing a native without the two natives. **In game 2026-10-03 (T9)** ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md) T9): binarytrees 0.4202 → 0.2521 s (0.600x), inside the pinned harness's prediction, and matmul 0.0946 → 0.0862 (0.911x), 2.2% better than it; mandelbrot 1.031x, not this change (under 800 allocator calls a run; 1.003 in the harness with the class the only difference, five interleaved runs a cell). Over the arena host with the accounting the game reads 1.011 / 1.014 / 1.064x: binarytrees' in-machine remainder, 0.183 s a run at T8, is 0.015 s. The new library's legacy path, the dropin's, costs nothing measurable (0.998x on binarytrees in the harness). **Done -- 2026-10-03:** the JNI crossing is measured and removed; the "row stays open" notes above are the interim record. **Status 2026-10-13:** the export counts and the `verifyModAssets` check above are the accounting change's; Value disposal added two more natives, so the additive now pins 91 `Java_*` / 93 PE names (the dropin unchanged at 87/89; `native/build-native.sh:904`, `:910`) and `verifyModAssets` requires four (`build.gradle.kts:163-168`) ([bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md](../bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md)). [accounting-sync.md](accounting-sync.md) records both.
- [x] **The C library's allocator is the largest standalone rung, and it is jnlua's convention, not ours — measured 2026-09-22** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-22.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-22.md) section 2). A state created through `lua_newstate` with a caller-supplied allocator never uses LuaJIT's own `lj_alloc`; jnlua supplies `realloc`/`free`, and so does our `lj52_newstate`, so every machine runs LuaJIT on the C library's allocator. Our binary in a bare C host on that allocator (no cap, no accounting) against the same binary as `luajit.exe`: sieve 2.24x, binarytrees 1.95x, matmul 1.30x, strings2 1.28x — the four benches that allocate (72 K to 14.08 M allocator calls per run) — and 0.96–1.05x on the four that do not; binarytrees pays 1.84x with the compiler off, so it is not the compiler's cost. The allocator is the leading candidate, not a proven cause: no arm ran the host on `lj_alloc`, and the host also differs from `luajit.exe` by the state record, the allocator dispatch and the eris link. Stock OC's PUC path is on the C library too, so this is a cost against a bare `luajit.exe`, not against what a player runs. Open: whether an `lj_alloc`-backed accounting allocator is possible under jnlua's `lua_setallocf` convention — the payoff on allocation-heavy OS code would be up to 2x. **Reproduced 2026-09-29 on the new box, idle** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md) section 2), now on disjoint cells: sieve 2.49x, binarytrees 1.92x, strings2 1.19x, matmul 1.15x; the four non-allocating benchmarks 0.88–1.02x; binarytrees 1.69x with the compiler off. Same standing: leading candidate, no `lj_alloc` arm run yet. **Answered 2026-10-02, and shipped in efc3d00: each machine's blocks now live in its own `lj_alloc` arena, under the unchanged accounting** ([bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md](../bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md)). `lj52_alloc` stays the state's allocf with its record as `ud`, so jnlua's convention is kept; its three memory touches go through `lj52_back`, which hands them to `lj_alloc_f` on an arena that `lj52_newstate` creates before the state and `lj52_close` destroys after `lua_close` — one allocator per block for its whole life. The missing arm, run standalone (min of 5 fresh processes, CHECKs identical across arms): the bare host on the arena against the 09-29 C-library host, each over `luajit.exe`, accounting off — sieve 2.647x → 1.000x, binarytrees 1.923x → 1.077x, strings2 1.203x → 1.023x, matmul 1.185x → 1.025x, the four non-allocating benchmarks unchanged; with the 64 MB accounting on, 1.012 / 1.222 / 1.062 / 1.049x. So the allocator was the rung, and what is left of it with the accounting off is at most 1.08x. In the machine (ocelot-brain launched pinned to the performance cores, the pinning row; JIT on, min of 5, the shipping sieve min of 4), the shipping DLL against the pre-arena one in one chain (23:07–23:14, 0 java processes at the start, java's affinity read back `0xC03C03` on every run): binarytrees 0.5671 → 0.4127–0.4295 s, matmul 0.1102 → 0.0954–0.0978, strings2 0.2737 → 0.2383–0.2509, sieve 0.1612 (21:45) → 0.1013, all on disjoint cells; mandelbrot 0.95x, sha256 0.99x, trampoline 1.009–1.027x (an earlier unfingerprinted gate run had read trampoline 1.064x; the prototype in its own fingerprinted chain 1.005–1.017x); nqueens' cells (0.43–1.12 s) too wide to read. Measured in game 2026-10-03 (T8; the dated note at the end of this row). Gates on the shipping natives (DLL `88b50796`, Linux `.so` `c1a9d865`, both warning-clean, serializer hash `8c5a1168` unchanged; jar `ocluajit-d20a14d-master+d20a14d376-dirty.jar`): wd 35/0, penalty 6/0, mem 29/0 (new M0/M0b/M0c: both states on the arena, at birth and at the end), race 2/0, security 37/0, shim_test 57/0, and the harness launched pinned: JIT on 57/0 (new `al-1-machine-heap-is-lj-alloc` PASS), JIT off 55/0, sieve 51/0, stock PUC 41/0 with `al-1` SKIP. `build-native.sh` now fails if `lj52shim.o` stops referencing the five arena symbols or if `LUAJIT_USE_SYSMALLOC` reaches the LuaJIT build. Every new check but the `LUAJIT_USE_SYSMALLOC` refusal (never run against a build with the flag) was seen to fail first: the symbol check finds 0 of 5 on the pre-arena object; M0/M0b/M0c and nothing else fail on the pre-arena object (heap = -1) and on a sabotaged shim whose arena is never created (heap = 0, every accounting check passing on the C-library fallback); `al-1` and nothing else fails on the pre-arena DLL. `_OCLJ_GCSTATS` returns a 14th value, `heap`: 1 arena, 0 C-library fallback, -1 no record. The trade-off, measured (the times transcribed in the session's data notes; no run log on disk): large-block churn runs ~1.44x slower than on the C library (400 × a 256 KB string and a 64K-slot array: 0.187–0.225 s on the arena host, as on `luajit.exe`, against 0.130–0.141 s), while small-block churn runs 1.17x faster (40 000 × a 4 KB string and a 512-slot array: 0.041–0.043 against 0.048–0.050 s). The candidate for the first, by the source: a request of 128 KB or more that the arena's free chunks and top cannot hold is mapped from the OS and unmapped on free (`alloc_sys`, `DEFAULT_MMAP_THRESHOLD`); no arm varied the block size or the threshold. From the review, not measured: an arena keeps roughly its peak size until its machine closes, memory one machine frees is no longer reusable by another, and each machine's first segment is 128 KB. A size split (blocks of 128 KB and more to the C library, chosen by `osize`) is a possible follow-up, not built (tracked since 2026-10-03 in the arena-costs row, with the resident-memory question just above). Not explained: one prototype run's seven-benchmark boot armed the emergency collector 54 times (the baseline and the other prototype run: 0; every collect completed, no refusals). **In game 2026-10-03 (T8)** ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md) T8; [bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md](../bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md#in-game-t8-2026-10-03)): on the shipping jar (the only `ocluajit` jar in `mods/`, native `88b50796`), in the same world and 16 MB machine as T7, CHECKs unchanged — binarytrees 0.5586 → 0.4202 s (0.752x), matmul 0.1031 → 0.0946 (0.918x), mandelbrot 0.0626 → 0.0637 (1.018x): the prediction from the pinned harness, met, each in-game min within the shipping DLL's two pinned-harness mins or 0.8% below them (matmul). Against plain LuaJIT the game now reads mandelbrot 0.98x, matmul 1.18x, binarytrees 2.19x (T7: 0.96 / 1.29 / 2.91x); over the arena host 0.98 / 1.11 / 1.77x, the remainder on binarytrees in the accounting-cost row. **Status 2026-10-10:** the 54-arm boot above is retired, with item (5) of the allocator loose-ends row: its 0-arm baseline no longer exists (that row's note).
- [ ] **Loose ends the allocator change turned up, 2026-10-02 — open.** (1) When `lua_newstate` on our allocator fails, `lj52_newstate` falls back to `luaL_newstate()` and hands back a state on LuaJIT's internal allocator with NO accounting record: `lj52_setallocf` finds no record and returns, so that machine runs with its RAM cap silently unenforced (`_OCLJ_GCSTATS` heap = -1; the harness's `b2` and `al-1` would fail). Pre-existing and kept as it was; the change rewrote only its comment, which now says so. Reaching it takes a non-GC64 `libluajit.a` (refused at stage 1b of `build-native.sh`) or memory running out. Whether it should return NULL instead — a machine that fails to start rather than one that runs uncapped — is open. (An arena that cannot be created is a different, accounted path: the state lives on the C library, heap = 0.) (2) `test/native/run.sh`, `run-race.sh` and `run-security.sh` still look for `$OCLJ_BUILD/obj` and `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit/src`, stale since the per-platform build move (`obj-$PLATFORM`, `luajit-$PLATFORM/src`, which `run-mem.sh` already reads); the 2026-10-02 gates ran shim_test, race and security through a scratch mirror of the old layout. `negative-control.sh` has the same stale paths. (3) 2026-10-03, run through a mirror for the accounting change: `negative-control.sh`'s memory lists had drifted since the trace flush (09-22) and are re-pinned; its `standinghook` control also fails on W10g, which postdates its list, on HEAD's tree too -- not investigated. **Status 2026-10-03:** All three are still open. `lj52shim.c:2300` still calls `luaL_newstate()`. `run.sh`, `run-race.sh`, `run-security.sh` and `negative-control.sh` still read `$OCLJ_BUILD/obj` and `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit/src` (e.g. `run-security.sh:29,42-43,51`), while `build-native.sh:407,419` writes `obj-$PLATFORM` and `luajit-$PLATFORM`. `standinghook` still expects only W6b W8c W12 (`negative-control.sh:472` in 2026-10-03's numbering; `:721` now). Correction to (1), by reading: on the shipped kernel the fallback state does not run uncapped. `_OCLJ_WATCHDOG` is installed regardless, but with no record `arm` raises `watchdog: not an lj52 state` (`lj52shim.c:1867`), so the kernel's main loop (`build/native/kernel/machine.lua:1639`) stops the machine before OpenOS runs. Only OC's own kernel, the packaging-fault fallback at `LuaJITArchitecture.java:154-166`, would run that state uncapped. Two more, from the 2026-10-03 review: (4) the `LUAJIT_USE_SYSMALLOC` refusal (`build-native.sh:524-528`) is the one check of the allocator change never seen to fail; run one build with the flag and watch it refuse. (5) One prototype run's seven-benchmark boot armed the emergency collector 54 times, unexplained, where the shipping DLL's gate boots read 0 ([bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md](../bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md):225-228); what is left is an explanation, or a recorded decision that it no longer matters. **Status 2026-10-04:** `negative-control.sh` derives the per-platform paths as `run-mem.sh` does and runs again, passing in full. Its `stopgap` list includes the new W5a-c. Otherwise (2) is unchanged: `run.sh`, `run-race.sh` and `run-security.sh` still read the old layout. **Status 2026-10-10:** (5) is retired, with the 54-arm sentence of the allocator row: its baseline, gate boots that armed 0 times at the harness's old ramScale 3.0, no longer exists, because at OC's 1.8 (bd302f2) every archived JIT-on seven-benchmark boot arms the collector hundreds of times or more (3760 on stage B, `bench/runs/2026-10-04-wall/logs/chainB/fullB3-additive-on.log:160`; 648 on the shipped DLL, `bench/runs/2026-10-05-reserve/logs/runsF/full/fullF-additive-on.log:163`). (3) is done (the 2026-10-04 status; `negative-control.sh:721` lists W10g). - [ ] **The arena's two unmeasured costs, added 2026-10-03 from the allocator row.** (1) **Resident memory across many machines.** By the source, not measured: on Windows an arena keeps roughly its peak size until its machine closes (`CALL_MUNMAP` releases only whole regions, `lj_alloc.c:192-210`; on Linux `alloc_trim` can return a top above the 2 MB trim threshold, `:1087-1124`), memory one machine frees cannot be reused by another, and each machine's first segment is 128 KB (`DEFAULT_GRANULARITY`, `lj_alloc.c:41`). No run has measured resident memory with more than one machine in a JVM ([bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md](../bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md):683-692, :780-783). Measure process RSS with N machines that peak and then idle, arena against the C library, before a server operator meets it. (2) **Large-block churn**, ~1.44x slower on the arena than on the C library, from timings with no run log on disk. The candidate, by the source, is `lj_alloc` mapping a request of 128 KB or more that the arena cannot hold straight from the OS and unmapping it on free (`DEFAULT_MMAP_THRESHOLD`, `lj_alloc.c:43`); no arm has varied the block size or the threshold. Measure that, then decide the size split the allocator row names (blocks of 128 KB and more to the C library, chosen by `osize`). **Status 2026-10-10:** Both still unmeasured: nothing in `test/` or `bench/` reads resident or working-set memory, and no build option selects the C library (`build-native.sh:611-619` refuses an object without the arena), so that arm needs a scratch build. The churn loops are uncommitted but still on disk in the 2026-10-02 session's scratchpad (`ljalloc/churnbench/`), md5-identical to `bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md:832-833`. By reading, the peak an arena keeps is not the cap: since THE CREDIT (f21a64b) a machine's heap may reach cap + G + 16 KiB, G being cap/16 within [32, 512] KiB (`lj52shim.c:1117-1118`, `:1145-1148`).
- [ ] **The sandbox + JVM residual is not separated below rung 4** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-22.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-22.md) section 5). The machine's RAM cap costs nothing measurable (the 3 457 941-byte tier vs 64 MB in the same host: 0.84–1.25x, the emergency collector armed 0 times in 160 runs, peak accounted use 795 KB against a 2.59 MB arm threshold). What remains between the capped host and the ocelot-brain machine, min of 5: pure loops mandelbrot 1.71x, sha256 1.81x, matmul 2.05x, sieve 1.28x (and the same factor with the JIT off — 1.66/1.82/1.96/1.58 — so it is not a per-trace cost); allocation-heavy binarytrees 2.91x, strings2 2.20x; trampoline 73.6x (in-machine compiled 0.7364 s equals in-machine interpreted 0.7389 s: the compiler cannot reach it); and nqueens **0.79x — faster in the machine than on every standalone arm** (1.353 s vs 1.68–1.89 s, spread 1.09x; the 09-04 sign reversal reproduces). Candidates the data cannot rank: OC's replaced builtins and OpenOS's patched globals, the per-resume watchdog arm/disarm, in-sandbox trace aborts (boot: 121 of 233 starts aborted; no per-benchmark count), the JVM thread, the JNI field get/set per allocation. The missing arms: a bare host on `lj_alloc` with accounting off; a host on jnlua's real JNI accessors under a JVM; the machine.lua sandbox over the same driver with no OpenOS and no JVM; an in-machine accounted-allocation count for trampoline. Cumulative, our LuaJIT inside a machine against plain LuaJIT: 1.63–2.90x on the pure loops and strings2, 6.41x on binarytrees, 81.8x on trampoline, 0.72x on nqueens. **Re-measured 2026-09-29 on the new box, quiesced** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md) section 5): the residual above the capped host is much smaller than the old box showed — mandelbrot 1.15x (was 1.71), sha256 1.39x (1.81), matmul 1.64x (2.05), binarytrees 1.76x (2.91), strings2 1.37x (2.20), trampoline 34.5x (73.6; the compiler now reaches part of it: 0.2756 s compiled against 0.3577 s interpreted in the machine), and sieve 0.84x and nqueens 0.65x FASTER in the machine (nqueens on a cell that spreads 2.38x). The old box's eight cores at 56–62% load ran the harness's poller beside the machine's executor and this box gives each its own core — the reading both documents support, with the CPU, GCC 16.2, the JDK build and the DLL build all changed at once and none separated. Cumulative against plain LuaJIT now: mandelbrot 1.15x, sha256 1.39x, matmul 2.0x, sieve 2.15x, binarytrees 3.7x, strings2 1.71x, trampoline 34x, nqueens 0.65x. Still unseparated; the in-game column (T7, same box) is next. **In-game 2026-10-02 (T7)** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md), "The in-game column"): in Minecraft on the same box mandelbrot reads 0.963x, matmul 1.052x and binarytrees 1.376x the capped host (0.963 / 1.289 / 2.909x plain LuaJIT), which is 0.839 / 0.641 / 0.782 of the harness machine's times: on this box, in this one run, the harness's rung 4 overstates what a player paid, and what the game paid above the capped host is smaller than the 1.15 / 1.64 / 1.76x above (binarytrees' in-game figure is its first rep only). Candidates for the harness's extra cost, none separated: core placement on the hybrid CPU (the harness JVM ran hidden in the background, the game in the foreground), the JVM's flags and collector, the harness's 25 ms poller, ocelot-brain 0.24.2 against OC 1.12.64-GTNH; re-running the harness with its JVM pinned to the performance cores would test the first. What remains in the game above the capped host is itself still unseparated. **Core placement, 2026-10-02: T7's candidates resolve to it** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md#addendum-2026-10-02-evening-the-harness-was-on-the-efficient-cores); the pinning row). The harness re-run with its JVM pinned to this hybrid CPU's performance cores (the java process's affinity read back 25 s into every run; DLL `bcf8715c`, all benchmarks, JIT on, 56/0) reads mandelbrot 0.0661, matmul 0.1049 and binarytrees 0.5168 s on tight cells — the game's 0.0626 / 0.1031 / 0.5586 s; pinned to the efficient cores it reads 0.0830 / 0.1629 / 0.8280, and unpinned 0.0889 / 0.1179 / 0.7760 on the widest cells. Pinned, the residual over the capped host (its 09-29 times) is mandelbrot 1.02x, sha256 1.03x, matmul 1.07x, strings2 1.18x, binarytrees 1.27x and trampoline 28x (28.5), against 1.15 / 1.39 / 1.64 / 1.37 / 1.76x and 34.5x unpinned on 09-29. The reading: unpinned, the machine thread of a hidden background JVM ran on efficient cores or migrated — the unpinned arm reads 1.12–1.50x the performance-core one, in the efficient-core arm's range but not benchmark by benchmark; which cores it actually ran on was not recorded. The JVM's flags, the 25 ms poller and ocelot-brain against GTNH OC were not tested separately and are not needed to explain the gap. Not explained: the JVM-hosted machine's sensitivity to the core type (efficient over performance 1.26x mandelbrot, 1.55x matmul, 1.60x binarytrees, against 1.05–1.11x for standalone `luajit.exe`). What remains pinned — 1.02–1.27x, and trampoline's 28x — is still unseparated, and the ladder has not been re-run on the arena (the allocator row), which moved the host and the machine alike. **In game on the arena, 2026-10-03 (T8)** ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md) T8; [bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md](../bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md#in-game-t8-2026-10-03)): against plain LuaJIT mandelbrot 0.98x, matmul 1.18x, binarytrees 2.19x (T7: 0.963 / 1.289 / 2.909x, above), and 0.98 / 1.11 / 1.77x over the arena host with the accounting; what binarytrees pays over that host is in the accounting-cost row. **In game without the JNI crossing, 2026-10-03 (T9)** ([bench/results-accounting-2026-10-03.md](../bench/results-accounting-2026-10-03.md#in-game-t9-2026-10-03)): against plain LuaJIT 1.011 / 1.078 / 1.313x, over the arena host with the accounting 1.011 / 1.014 / 1.064x; what is left above that host, 0.015 s a binarytrees run against a cold-process baseline from another session, is not separated. **Status 2026-10-03:** Two of the four missing arms now exist. One is the bare host on `lj_alloc` with accounting off: r3ul in [bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md](../bench/results-allocator-2026-10-02.md), "Standalone". The other is jnlua's real accessors under a plain JVM: `JniArm`, in that document's JNI-crossing section. With core placement, the arena (T8) and the JNI crossing (removed in 6edc5e0, T9) separated, the game pays 1.011 / 1.014 / 1.064x over the arena host with the accounting. Still unseparated: binarytrees' last 0.015 s a run, and trampoline's ~28x (0.222 s in the pinned harness machine against 0.008 s in r3l; the accounting change left it at 1.00). Also unexplained, and missing from this list until the 2026-10-03 review: sieve's in-machine cost, about 210 ns per accounted call over its 72.5 K, with the emergency collector running 640–800 times a run (the accounting-cost row; [bench/results-accounting-2026-10-03.md](../bench/results-accounting-2026-10-03.md):121-122 rules out the JNI crossing); and nqueens reading faster in the machine than standalone (the re-called-chunk row, next; candidate, untested: LuaJIT's own self-flush landing mid-run). That row's account that one flush after a prototype's third closure ends the climb is also untested, though the flush policy itself is a recorded no. By reading, trampoline's candidate is the sandbox `pcall`, which runs `pcall(checkDeadline)` and its `computer.realTime()` upcall before every protected call (`build/native/kernel/machine.lua:808-813`, `:76-77`). The machine.lua-without-OpenOS-and-JVM arm and an in-machine allocation count for trampoline were never run. The count is now cheap: `_OCLJ_GCSTATS`' 19th value counts allocator calls, and the harness already takes its difference for `acc-2` (`OcljSmoke.scala:5502`). **Status 2026-10-10:** Trampoline's candidate is measured standalone, not only read: on a replica of the sandbox `pcall` LuaJIT pays 8.78x a plain `pcall` with the JIT on (lua5.3 1.72x), and a Lua function in place of the clock call restores the plain time (`bench/oc/trampoline.lua:61-78`, fb54521); the missing arm is the in-machine split, a kernel whose `checkDeadline` skips the `computer.realTime()` upcall beside the stock wrapper. Trampoline has not moved through the wall work, 0.2218–0.2300 s in all 19 pinned additive JIT-on suites in `bench/runs` from 2026-10-03 to 10-05, and on the final build stock PUC 5.2 reads 0.4242 s against our 0.2251 s, 1.88x, where mandelbrot reads 12.6x (`bench/runs/2026-10-05-reserve/logs/runsF/full/fullF-stock.log`, `fullF-additive-on.log`). Binarytrees' 0.015 s is T9's, from before the wall work: the pinned harness's binarytrees min reads 0.2586–0.3007 s in the 10 suites run with the site-13 kernel (85faa0e; C0, site 13 on stage B's DLLs, already reads 0.2671, `bench/runs/2026-10-04-wall/README.md`) against 0.2457–0.2590 s in the 9 before, one suite per build and not a timing study, so re-measure before quoting a remainder.
- [x] **Re-calling one loaded chunk ran binarytrees ~2x slower from the third call, and 1.4–1.6x slower than with the compiler off — the trace state the earlier calls leave behind, not the closure count; a `jit.flush()` before each call removes the climb (every call within 8% of the first). Recorded 2026-10-02; upstream LuaJIT behaviour, nothing of ours to fix.** Found through T7's load-once runner and reproduced standalone ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md), "The in-game column"): binarytrees called as one loaded chunk in one VM ran 0.198–0.202 s, then 0.292–0.302 s, then 0.370–0.400 s from the third call on (our `luajit.exe`; pristine upstream 0.199–0.200, 0.300–0.311, 0.391–0.406; the bare C host 0.419–0.440, 0.522–0.559, 0.656–0.673, with a further, unexplained step from rep 6 (r3 only, the C-library bare host, [bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md):1078-1084; moot for the shipping native since the arena, efc3d00)), against 0.256–0.267 s on `luajit.exe` and 0.258–0.266 s upstream with the compiler off through the same driver in a later run (the host's own `--joff` run read 0.428–0.589 s) (where unflushed `luajit.exe` ran 0.383–0.388 s from the third call, 1.43–1.52x), while loading the file afresh for every call stayed flat (0.193–0.210 on `luajit.exe`). The separating test: the same loaded chunk with `jit.flush()` before every call ran 0.191–0.209 s on every call on `luajit.exe` and upstream, past LuaJIT's three-closure threshold (`PROTO_CLC_POLY`, `lj_obj.h:409-411`; `lj_record.c:802`, `:1795`) or not, so the closure count does not make the 2x slow code (calls 3–8 at 1.01–1.08x the first: a small step at the threshold, its cause not separated); the climb needs the state the flush clears — the earlier calls' traces and machine code and the penalty cache, not the hot counters, which `lj_trace_flushall` leaves — and the traces are the candidate, since calls 4–7 record nothing yet run slower than the interpreter and a penalty can only keep code interpreted. The account, not measured (no `-jdump`; `texit` cannot see linked exits; the trace callback did not record parents): later calls enter call 1's closure-specialised traces and fail their guards into the 19–27 traces calls 2 and 3 added (most likely side traces) or back to the interpreter, and the counter is why recording stops after the third call. OpenOS's shell loads a program afresh on every run, so re-running a program from the shell does not hit this; a program that re-creates a function in one VM while traces for its earlier closures are attached — a local function defined inside a loop or a hot function, a chunk loaded once and called repeatedly — can, on upstream LuaJIT as on ours (measured on binarytrees only; by the account, not a function containing bit-operator syntax, which reads as polymorphic from its first closure). Until 2026-09-22 the persist-side flush cleared such trees at essentially every save (it fired whenever `persist()` reached a thread parked inside a generic-for loop; the "JIT x persist interaction" row), and by the account one flush after a prototype's third closure ends the climb for it (untested); since its removal they last until the machine's VM is replaced (a reboot, or a reload into a new VM) or something flushes: memory pressure, or LuaJIT itself at 1000 traces or a full 2 MB mcode reserve (the `jit.opt` row). Recorded, not acted on: an occasional flush would trade recompiles for this, and nqueens (`solve` defined inside its rep loop) is an untested lead for its compiled-slower-than-interpreted reading outside the machine (0.82–0.83x), one that must also explain why the same file gains 1.61x from the compiler inside it. `bench/oc/ingame-ladder.lua` now loads the file for every rep, as the harness suite does; the copy on the in-game disk is still the load-once one. **The nqueens lead, confirmed 2026-10-02** ([bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md](../bench/results-ladder-2026-09-29.md#nqueens-the-same-mechanism)): `bench/oc/nqueens.lua` defines `local function solve` inside its 6-rep loop — a new closure over new tables every rep, recursing through its own upvalue. Per-rep times, 3 fresh processes per cell, CHECK 85200 in all 24 runs: as written, rep 1 0.057–0.060 s, rep 2 0.087–0.089, reps 3–6 0.226–0.237 (total 1.055–1.091 s, our build and upstream alike); with `-joff` every rep 0.143–0.146 (total 0.865–0.871); with `jit.flush()` before each rep, reps 3–6 0.076–0.082 (total 0.427–0.435); with one `solve` for all reps (its upvalues now mutable), every rep 0.056–0.066 (total 0.347–0.360). So its compiled-slower-than-interpreted reading (0.82–0.83x) is this row's mechanism: rep 1 compiled is 2.4x faster than interpreted, and from rep 3 the reps run 1.6x slower than interpreted on the traces earlier reps left; hoisted, the whole benchmark is 2.5x faster than the interpreter, on upstream as on ours. Unlike binarytrees, the flushed reps still step up at the third closure — reps 3–6 at ~1.3x rep 1 (0.077 against 0.059 s; flushed rep 2 0.057–0.060) — a step that sits at LuaJIT's three-closure threshold (`PROTO_CLC_POLY`); the closure count is the candidate for that ~1.3x, not separated from whatever else changes at the third rep, and what the flush removes is ~3x (reps 3–6 0.226–0.237 s unflushed against 0.076–0.082 flushed) of the whole climb's ~3.9x over rep 1. Still open: why the same file reads faster inside the machine (six seven-benchmark runs launched pinned on 2026-10-02, mins 0.43–0.56 s and maxes 1.09–1.14 s; 0.68 s unpinned on 09-29); candidate, untested: LuaJIT's own self-flush (1000 traces or 2 MB mcode) landing mid-run in a machine that already holds the boot's traces. **In game, 2026-10-03 (T8)** ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md) T8): with the per-rep runner now on the disk, binarytrees' five reps in game read 0.420–0.432 s (1.03x), where T7's load-once runner climbed to 1.68x the first — flat, as standalone fresh loads on `lj_alloc` were; the DLL changed in between too (the arena), but standalone the climb appeared on `lj_alloc` and the C library alike.
- [x] **`lua_pushcfunction` memoisation — DONE** (`native/lj52shim.c`), together with the no-refuse window described above. On 5.2 the push stores a *light* C function: no allocation, cannot fail, and pushing the same pointer twice yields equal values — which is what makes JNLua's `*_protected` discipline airtight and what a perms table wants. LuaJIT has no such type, so the shim memoises one `GCfunc` per (state, C function) in `registry[3]`: measured 0 bytes over 20 000 warm pushes, and identity restored.
- [x] **`lua_load` mode escape hatches DELETED, with a regression test that has teeth** (`test/native/security_test.c`, 37 checks; `test/native/negative-control.sh` rebuilds the shim from sabotaged copies and asserts the exact failing-id set, so the suite is *observed* to fail rather than merely observed to pass). The consolidation also found two defects in the byte-sniffer variant nobody had recorded: mode `"b"` against a TEXT chunk was silently accepted, and the reject path leaked a stack slot per refused chunk.
- [x] **`build-native.sh`'s collector gate had been vacuous since 2026-09-15 — found and fixed 2026-09-22.** `codegrep()` ran plain `grep -n` (BRE) with the pattern `lj_gc_fullgc|lj_gc_step|luaC_|lua_gc *\(|LUA_GCCOLLECT|LUA_GCSTEP`; BRE `grep` dies on the unbalanced `\(` ("Unmatched ( or \\("), stderr was discarded, it matched nothing, and `| grep -q .` passed on every build. The gate that forbids the shim from calling the collector never ran once. Now `grep -nE`, proven both ways (the corrected form finds a sabotaged `lj_gc_step(L); lua_gc(L, LUA_GCCOLLECT, 0);` line and nothing in the real shim; the old form prints the grep error and no line), and the 2026-09-22 native build was the first in which it actually ran — it passed. `run-mem.sh` was stale in the same way (it looked for `obj/` and `luajit/` paths that had not existed since the per-platform move) and `negative-control.sh` still references `$OCLJ_BUILD/obj/eris_lj.o`. The lesson is the one already in the memory file, applied to gates written before it: **a check that has never been seen to fail has never been seen to run.**
- [ ] **Make the mode gate's BUILD assertion behavioural.** `native/build-native.sh` greps for the macro's *shape* and runs no test; the behavioural coverage exists but is opt-in. Link `security_test.c`'s load probe into a postflight stage. **Status 2026-10-03:** Still open. The gate is the `grep -q` at `native/build-native.sh:391`, and stage 6 (postflight, :786) checks exports and runs no test binary. The behavioural checks are `security_test.c`'s MG section (37/0 at the 10-03 accounting gates) and the harness's g1/g1b/g2/g3 (`OcljSmoke.scala:2102-2142`), **which test our shim only in the dropin arm** (corrected 2026-10-03): `bytecodeGate()` builds its state from `LuaStateFactory.Lua52` (`:2084`), and in the additive arm that is OC's bundled PUC native, so there they test PUC's `lua_load`. In the additive (shipping) arm nothing tests the shim's mapping: `d2-sandbox-bytecode-gate` checks the other enforcement point, the sandbox's base `load` inside a booted machine, and a shim that drops `lua_load`'s mode still passes it (`OcljSmoke.scala:4425-4432`). Also open: move `bytecodeGate()` onto `armFactory`, as the memory probes were moved on 2026-10-03 (`:1736-1740`), and see g1 fail on an additive build whose shim drops the mode. The script that runs the first, `run-security.sh`, still reads the stale `obj/` and `luajit/src` paths (loose end (2) of the 10-02 item above), so today it runs only through a mirror of the old layout. **Status 2026-10-10:** Still open. The `37/0` counts the whole `security_test` (FX 2, MG 17, SB 6, ER 4, LE 8); MG is 17/17 at the 10-05 gates (`bench/runs/2026-10-05-errmem/logs/gates/security.log`). The g1 text bears out the 10-03 correction: in the 25 full-suite runs of `bench/runs/2026-10-05-*` the additive arms refuse 15/15 with PUC's `attempt to load a binary chunk (mode is 't')`, as stock does 5/5, and the dropin 5/5 with LuaJIT's `attempt to load chunk with wrong mode`. The fail-first build cannot use the negative control's `dropmode`: `build-native.sh` refuses it at the shape grep (`negctl.log:202-205` in the same gates), so it needs a sabotage the grep accepts (by reading, a macro that keeps `lua_loadx` and passes `NULL` for the mode would pass it) or a bypass. Both variants compile into one `obj-$PLATFORM` (`native/build-native.sh:407`), so a postflight linking `security_test.c` tests the variant just built, but through the macro re-expanded in the test's own unit (`test/native/run-security.sh:45-49`), not the DLL's `jnlua.o` (`native/build-native.sh:694-696`); only g1 on `armFactory` (`OcljSmoke.scala:1819-1821`) reaches that.
- [x] ~~Delete both `lua_load` mode escape hatches~~ — done above. OC reads `computer.lua.allowBytecode` and passes `"t"` to refuse precompiled chunks, so mode is **security-relevant**. LuaJIT 2.1 exports `lua_loadx` with the same whitelist semantics, so the gate is fully implementable — but one shim carries a build-conditional fallback that silently discards mode and another honours an `OCLJ_NOMODECHECK` env var. Either shipped by accident turns `allowBytecode=false` into a lie with no error, no log line and no failing test. Test: `load(string.dump(f))` must be refused under `allowBytecode=false`. Note the serializer legitimately loads LuaJIT bytecode via `lj_bcwrite`/`lua_loadx`, so the `"b"` path stays open internally while shut to sandbox code. **Done -- verified 2026-10-03:** `native/build-native.sh:279-310` refuses to build when `OCLJ_NOMODECHECK`, `LJ52_DROP_LOAD_MODE` or any of eleven other hatch tokens appears on a non-comment line of the shim. `:391` requires `lj52shim.h:138` to map `lua_load` onto `lua_loadx`, and `:387` refuses an `eris_lj.c` that includes the shim header, so the serializer's own `lua_loadx(..., "b")` (eris_lj.c:1894) stays open while sandbox code is shut out. The test asked for is harness milestone `d2-sandbox-bytecode-gate` (OcljSmoke.scala:4425: `load(string.dump(f))` and `load(dump, name, "bt")` refused inside booted OpenOS under `allowBytecode=false`), and the C entry point is covered by `g1-bytecode-refused-with-mode-t`, `security_test.c` MG1–MG9 and `negative-control.sh`'s `dropmode` variant, which must fail exactly MG1 MG3 MG9 (negative-control.sh:167-151).
- [ ] **A C-recursion ceiling.** LuaJIT has no `nCcalls`/`LUAI_MAXCCALLS`; `lua_resume` takes no `from` and does no C-call accounting, so 5.2's protection against C-stack exhaustion by nested resume does not exist. Sandbox-reachable process kill. Not fixable in the shim — an explicit depth counter above jnlua, naturally in the CHECKHOOK watchdog.
- [x] **Unify on the differentially-tested `lua_compare`.** The shim that booted OpenOS implements `LUA_OPLE` as `!lua_lessthan(idx2,idx1)` — 5.1 semantics, wrong for a metatable defining only `__le`. A different shim implements it via a Lua helper and measured `__le`-only tables exact. So the build that has booted a machine is the one whose comparison semantics were *not* tested. **Done -- verified 2026-10-03:** one shim remains (`native/lj52shim.c` holds the only tracked `lua_compare`), and it uses the helper form: every op runs through the VM's own `==`/`<`/`<=` in the registry-cached `cmp` chunk (lj52shim.c:100-104, :2414), so `LUA52COMPAT`'s 5.2 `__le` rules apply. It is covered by `security_test.c` LE1–LE8 (an `__le`-only metatable in both polarities, `__le` and `__lt` disagreeing, `__le` fired once and `__lt` never, numeric controls) and by `shim_test.c` section A. `negative-control.sh:258-242` rebuilds the shim with `!lua_lessthan(L, idx2, idx1)` put back and requires exactly LE1–LE4 to fail.
- [x] `bit32` differentially covered for VALUES — 45/47 exact against real 5.2 across shifts >= 32, negative displacements, `arshift` sign extension and saturation, rotate masking, operand wrap and string coercion. The 2 divergences are the error-message *function-name* field only (`'?'` vs `'extract'`), which stock LuaJIT does for `string.rep` and `math.floor` too. Fix the claim in the docs, not the code.
- [x] **Decide `luaL_requiref` deliberately, and move `_VERSION` out of `luaopen_eris`.** The shim implements 5.3 semantics (short-circuit when `_LOADED[modname]` is truthy); real 5.2 always calls `openf`. Benign today only because the skipped `luaopen_coroutine` is a no-op — but `luaopen_eris` is the ONLY site setting `_VERSION = "Lua+Eris 5.2"`, which is the string the anti-vacuity LuaJ guard reads. A single point of failure that can now be bypassed with no error. **Status 2026-10-03:** neither change is made. `luaL_requiref` (lj52shim.c:2505-2525) still returns early when `_LOADED[modname]` is truthy, under a comment that calls this 5.2's behaviour, which PUC 5.2.4 contradicts (lauxlib.c:886-899 calls `openf` unconditionally), and `_VERSION = "Lua+Eris 5.2"` is still set only in `luaopen_eris` (lj52shim.c:2757-2758). It is still benign: LuaJIT's `luaopen_base` registers `_LOADED.coroutine` (lib_base.c:706), so only jnlua's COROUTINE open short-circuits, and the shim's `luaopen_coroutine` (:2564) would return that same table. The guard premise is gone on our arms: no anti-vacuity check there reads `_VERSION`, since the harness refuses a run on the `_OCLJ_NATIVE` marker, the `LuaStateLuaJIT` class, the `jit` table and the eris library (OcljSmoke.scala:1656-1680); only the stock arm's weak `contains("5.2")` test reads it (`:1699-1700`), where the string comes from OC's own native. And a host left at LuaJIT's "Lua 5.1" would still give the sandbox "Lua 5.2" (kernel:857). **Retired 2026-10-10:** neither change has an effect anything can observe. On our VM the host `_VERSION` is read only by the kernel, which derives `isLuaOver54`, `isLuaOver53` and the sandbox's copy from it (OC machine.lua:65-66, :812; kernel:106-107, :857), and "Lua+Eris 5.2" and LuaJIT's "Lua 5.1" give the same three values; the harness prints it but identifies our VM by marker, class, `jit` and eris (OcljSmoke.scala:1601, :1656-1680). The short-circuit skips only COROUTINE, whose opener would return the same table (lib_base.c:706; lj52shim.c:2564-2575). The comment that called the short-circuit 5.2's behaviour (lj52shim.c:2506-2509) now says it is 5.3's (2026-10-10).
- [ ] **Cross-build blob compatibility.** Restoring the `jit` table adds 9 permanents reachable from `_G` (163 vs 154), so a blob written by a pre-merge native and one written by the canonical native have different perms key sets. Untested: what a restore across that boundary actually does. Decide whether a build stamp is warranted so a mid-world upgrade fails loudly rather than strangely. **Status 2026-10-03:** a build stamp exists, but it does not cover the case this item names. Blobs carry LuaJIT commit | `LUAJIT_VERSION` | a hash of `eris_lj.c`+`eris_lj.h` (eris_lj.c:101; build-native.sh:756-771), and a mismatch is refused with "build fingerprint mismatch" (eris_lj.c:3171-3176), which `Machine.load` logs before it closes the machine (OC Machine.scala:808-813). The shim, where the perms set changes (`luaopen_jit`, the `_OCLJ_*` globals), is outside the hash, so across a shim-only change a blob is checked only name by name: a permanent the restoring build lacks fails with "unknown permanent", one that changed type fails with "permanent changed type" (eris_lj.c:2857-2861; serializer/tests/m1.lua:288-297), and everything else restores without a check. Open: no test feeds a mismatched fingerprint (m1.lua:333-351 covers only the magic, the checksum and the format byte) or restores across a perms-set change, and whether to hash the shim into the fingerprint is undecided. **The kernel is outside the fingerprint too** (2026-10-03 review): `build/native/kernel/machine.lua` is hashed nowhere, a restored machine keeps the kernel it was saved with (the `_ENV` row's deployment note), and our bundled-roots `load` asserts the patched kernel's synchronized-call shape (the `W6b` row). So a mod update that changes that shape can meet an old restored kernel. Decide the kernel with the shim: hash it into the fingerprint, or detect a mismatch and force a reboot, and tell players either way. **Status 2026-10-10:** both uncovered cases have now occurred under one fingerprint: since 10-03 the shim gained the permanent `_OCLJ_WALLSTATS` (d705515; lj52shim.c:2156-2157) and the kernel gained site 13 (85faa0e), while serializer/ is unchanged since 83aeabd, so the 10-05 builds still stamp 1ee778a4 and serializer hash 8c5a1168 (bench/runs/2026-10-05-errmem/logs/build-additive.log:5, :37). Neither test above exists yet (grep, 2026-10-10). The same blind spot at packaging time, `verifyModAssets` checking the serializer hash and two exports but not the shim, and the kernel only for presence (build.gradle.kts:123-186), is the jar staleness gate row. **Status 2026-10-13:** another change of the shim's permanent set under the same fingerprint: Value disposal ([bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md](../bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md)) adds the raw global `_OCLJ_VGCSTATS`, a C function set by `lj52_wd_install` beside `_OCLJ_GCSTATS` and `_OCLJ_WALLSTATS` (`native/lj52shim.c:2504-2505`; `_OCLJ_WALLSTATS` is now at `:2502-2503`), so a new permanent reachable from `_G`, the same class of change as `_OCLJ_WALLSTATS` (d705515). It is a separate global, not five more `_OCLJ_GCSTATS` positions, because two gates pin that function's 20 values (`:2470-2472`). serializer/ is unchanged, and the new builds still stamp serializer hash 8c5a1168 (`bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/logs/builds/build-additive.log:38`). Not tested across builds, and this change adds neither test above. Also since this change, `verifyModAssets` checks the serializer hash and four exports, not two (`build.gradle.kts:123-149`, `:151-184`), and still the kernel only for presence (`:186-195`). The fingerprint cite above, `build-native.sh:756-771`, is now `:785-800`. **Status 2026-10-16:** the fingerprint gained a fourth field, the VM-features word, which `serializer/eris_lj.c` derives from the LuaJIT macros it is compiled with: seven tokens, `ffi`, `lua52`, `gc64`, `fr2`, `dn`, `le`/`be`, `p64`/`p32`; shipped: `ffi0.lua52.gc64.fr2.dn0.le.p64`. A mismatch is refused at the header, before the checksum and before any object is restored, naming the field that differs (`build fingerprint mismatch: VM features differs (blob '...', this build '...')`). `serializer/tests/fingerprint.lua` is the first test that feeds a mismatched fingerprint (16 checks; on HEAD's serializer 9 run, the seven token checks having no word to flip, and 5 fail; a copy with the compare removed fails 9, `serializer/tests/negctl-fingerprint.sh`). Before it, FFI-on and FFI-off builds of one serializer stamped the same fingerprint, and the FFI-off build restored the other's blobs, recipes included, failing only mid-body on an FFI prototype (measured 2026-10-16). Still outside the fingerprint: the shim, the kernel and the LuaJIT patches in `native/luajit/`, i.e. this row's two open cases and the jar-staleness row's. A LuaJIT patch that changes the bytecode or the dump format must also set `BCDUMP_VERSION` to 0x80 or above, as `lj_bcdump.h:36-37` asks, and add a token to the word. The `eris_lj.c` cites above moved with the word: `:101` is now `:165`, `:2857-2861` is `:2931-2935`, `:3171-3176` is `:3297-3302`. **Status 2026-10-17:** the VM-features word gained an eighth token, `pack1`, the first that names a library rather than a layout: `string.pack`, `string.unpack` and `string.packsize` are three C functions within reach of `_G`, so three more permanents (135 -> 138 in a scratch walk of `_G` over OC's library set). It is derived in `eris_lj.c` from `OCLJ_LIB_STRPACK` in `native/luajit/oclj_features.h`; `oclj_strpack.c` refuses to compile without that macro; `build-native.sh` observes `string.pack` on luajit.exe and fails a word that disagrees; and `serializer/tests/fingerprint.lua` checks the token against the archive's string library (`negctl-fingerprint.sh` arm `packword`: an eris compiled without the macro fails exactly that check). Measured in scratch, 3 runs alike (r2 synth `logs/xbuild-matrix.out`), with one serializer hash throughout: WITHOUT the token, a blob saved with `string.pack` failed mid-restore on a build without it ("unknown permanent"), and a blob saved without it RESTORED on a build with it, the sandbox's `string.pack` nil while `('<i2'):pack(258)` worked through the host metatable, until a reboot; WITH the token both crossings are refused at the header ("VM features differs"). `eris_lj.c` changed, so the serializer hash moved too: every earlier blob is refused once, by name, before any release. Still outside the fingerprint: the shim's permanents (`_OCLJ_*`), the kernel, and the LuaJIT patches that change neither a permanent nor the bytecode; a future fork addition gets its own token the same way. The general answer to this row's first question, a hash of the sorted permanent names carried in the header, stays open. **Status 2026-10-17, round 3:** the word gained a ninth token, `bit64` (else `bit32`, upstream's behaviour), the first that names a change to what LuaJIT's bytecode MEANS rather than a layout or a library: a folded constant of `& | ~ << >>` is now a 64-bit result (`return 1 << 32` dumps 4294967296; on the round-2 build, 1), and a restored thread's operators compute by the new rules. It is derived in `eris_lj.c` from `OCLJ_OPS_BIT64` in `native/luajit/oclj_features.h`. The same change sets `BCDUMP_VERSION` to 0x80 (`native/luajit/patch-bitop-bcdump.sh`; `lj_bcdump.h:36-39` asks for 0x80 or above for a private change, and the next one takes 0x81), so an upstream dump is refused (`security_test.c` FF12), and `eris_lj.c` refuses to compile with the macro on and the version below 0x80. Upstream's `BCDUMP_F_BITOP` could not stand in: it does not mark folded constants (`return 1 << 32` dumps without it; round 3's compat investigator, 2026-10-17). `build-native.sh` observes `tonumber("1") << 32` on luajit.exe, from a runtime operand because constants fold, and fails a word that disagrees; `serializer/tests/fingerprint.lua` checks the token against the archive's operators (`negctl-fingerprint.sh` arm `bitword`); and `build.gradle.kts` now compares the whole word, `<hash>|ffi0.lua52.gc64.fr2.dn0.le.p64.pack1.bit64`, not a suffix, so the middle tokens are checked too. Measured on round 3's first prototype, version bumped but no token yet: a blob of closures saved on the round-2 build was refused mid-restore (`eris-lj: cannot load function (eris-lj: cannot load incompatible bytecode)`), and the reverse likewise, each build restoring its own; with the token, the refusal moves to the header (measured on the compat investigator's own prototype, not re-run on the shipped build). `eris_lj.c` changed, so the serializer hash moved, to `412be694`: every earlier blob is refused once, at the header, before any release. **Status 2026-10-19:** the commit field could differ between our own release natives, and the glibc round settled it before the release build. HEAD 3adfeaf's `build-native.sh` read `LJ_COMMIT` with `git -C "$OCLJ_LUAJIT" rev-parse` or wrote `unknown`, and AlmaLinux 8, where the release .so is now built (the glibc row), then had no git, so the round's first Alma builds (`62555188`, `f357129a`) stamped `unknown|LuaJIT 2.1.ROLLING|412be694|...` where the Windows DLL stamps `1ee778a4|...` [M: the harness's `GUARD VM FINGERPRINT` lines in those builds' first Linux arms]. `eris_lj.c` compares the whole fingerprint at the header (`ERIS_LJ_FINGERPRINT`, `:204`; the compare at `:3340`), so such a pair would refuse each other's blobs [S; not run]. Now `git_head` (`build-native.sh:544-559`, `LJ_COMMIT` at `:561`) reads the checkout's own `.git` with git, or `.git/HEAD` and its ref file or `packed-refs` line when git cannot, and the build refuses to run without a commit (`:562-565`, "cannot read the LuaJIT checkout's commit") [S]. Fail-firsts in AlmaLinux 8 (`bench/runs/2026-10-19-glibc/failfirst/ff-alma/summary.txt`) [M]: HEAD's script with git failing stamps `unknown` (`head`); the current one with git failing and no `.git` is refused (`nogit`); with git failing and `.git/HEAD` readable it stamps `1ee778a4`, and its .so is byte-identical to the one built with git (`relfile` against `release`, both `6c888050`). AlmaLinux 8 has git now too (2.43.7, `logs/build-alma/host.txt`). The release .so carries `1ee778a4|LuaJIT 2.1.ROLLING|412be694|ffi0.lua52.gc64.fr2.dn0.le.p64.pack1.bit64`, and the harness's guard reads that same string from the Windows DLL, the Alma-built .so in AlmaLinux 8 and the same .so in Ubuntu [M: `logs/build-alma/glibc.txt`; `eris.version` in `logs/harness-win/gl-additive-on-a`, `harness-alma/lx-additive-on-a`, `harness-ubuntu/additive-on-a`]. So a blob passes the fingerprint between a Windows and a Linux release native; no blob has been restored across the two [I; not run]. The open item in the glibc row is closed. **Status 2026-10-22:** the refusal has been seen in game. T11's session ([in-game-tests.md](in-game-tests.md)) loaded a world last saved under a4994e6's jar (serializer hash `8c5a1168`) on 19a7806's (`412be694`): our native refused the computer's state at the header, naming the first field that differs and both values (`eris-lj: build fingerprint mismatch: serializer hash differs (blob '8c5a1168', this build '412be694'); the blob is from a different natives build`), and OC logged it as `Unexpected error loading a state of computer` [M: `bench/runs/2026-10-22-t11-game/log-excerpts.txt`], leaving the computer stopped (OC `Machine.scala`:808-813 in 1.12.58-GTNH [S]). Started again, it booted fresh and ran T11 [I: that directory's results file exists; a refused state leaves no saved kernel]. So a mid-world upgrade across a fingerprint change fails cleanly: refused at the header by name and logged, not restored wrongly. What the player is told is still open (the 2026-10-03 note's "tell players either way"): nothing in the game says why the computer is off [I], and the only words are in the log, where OC's line asks for a bug report ("Unless you're upgrading/downgrading across a major version, please report this!"). **Status 2026-10-23:** the counterpart of the 2026-10-22 note's refusal, a blob from the same build, was accepted in game. The 2026-10-23 game session loaded the world T11's 2026-10-22 session had saved, under the same jar (19a7806), and logged no `eris-lj` text and no `Unexpected error loading a state` [M: `bench/runs/2026-10-23-t11-game/`]. The computer was running when the user reached it [M: the user's report], whereas a refused state leaves it stopped (OC `Machine.scala`:808-813 in 1.12.58-GTNH [S]), so the state was restored [I], and T11's LuaJIT arm then ran and passed on it.
- [ ] **The jar build cannot see a stale shim, LuaJIT patch or kernel -- found 2026-10-05, added 2026-10-10.** `verifyModAssets` (`build.gradle.kts:76-188`) refuses a library in OC's own name (`:96-107`), a native whose bytes lack the serializer's hash (`:133-149`) and one without the two accounting exports (`:157-175`); of the kernel it reports only the size (`:177-186`). Nothing ties a packaged native to `lj52shim.c`, `native/jnlua/patch-resume-error.sh` or the two `native/luajit` patch scripts, or the kernel to `patch-machine-lua.lua`. So the 2026-10-03 Linux .so (`90be1300`) stayed in `dist/` through stages A-C and THE WINDOW ([bench/results-errmem-2026-10-05.md](../bench/results-errmem-2026-10-05.md):25-27), and `build/libs/ocluajit-85faa0e-master+85faa0e99e-dirty.jar` (2026-10-04) packs it beside the stage-C DLL `05d133cf` (results-wall-2026-10-04.md:569) and kernel `1d12c322` (verified 2026-10-10). The serializer check answered the same failure on 2026-09-17 (the stale-native row). What remains: a source hash in each native and in the kernel, or a manifest beside them, refused on a mismatch per platform and shown failing first on a stale .so. Decide it with the blob-compatibility row: its fingerprint holds the LuaJIT commit, its version and the serializer's hash, not the shim's or the kernel's, and a hash added there would also be this check's fragment. Also, from the licensing row: a check that the root and resource copies of `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` stay identical. **Status 2026-10-13:** `verifyModAssets` is now `build.gradle.kts:76-197`, and it requires the four oclj exports `LuaStateLuaJIT` declares, not two: the accounting's two and Value disposal's two, `ocljInstallValueFinalizer` and `ocljValueGcCounters` (the check `:151-184`, its list `:163-168`, the refusal `:173-182`; [bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md](../bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md)). The own-name and serializer checks are unchanged at `:96-107` and `:133-149`; the kernel's size report is now `:186-195`. The new names refuse one stale native, a build from before Value disposal (aa12418's DLL `45eb443b` holds neither, `bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/logs/builds/exportgate-proof.txt`), which the serializer check passes, since serializer/ did not change (hash still 8c5a1168). The row's case stands: nothing ties a native to `lj52shim.c`, and a shim change that adds no export still passes. **Status 2026-10-16:** `verifyModAssets` now requires the serializer hash followed by the VM-features word's first two tokens, `<hash>|ffi0.lua52.`, so a native built with FFI or without LUA52COMPAT is refused too; the shim, the LuaJIT patches and the kernel are still not tied to the jar.
- [x] Counting allocator wired to `recomputeMemory` — done as above; the state is created via `lua_newstate` on GC64 and its allocator is never swapped, which is what makes the per-state record recoverable through `lua_getallocf`.
- [x] Kernel: run GTNH machine.lua with a `bit32` shim over LuaJIT's `bit`; sandbox reports `_VERSION = "Lua 5.2"`; `persistKey` nil path (LuaJ semantics) **Superseded -- 2026-10-03:** each of the three parts was replaced by a design that ships. `bit32` is not a shim over LuaJIT's `bit` but a real 5.2 `bit32` in C (lj52shim.c:2579-2733; aliasing was rejected because BitOp's results are signed), opened by jnlua's BIT32 openlib, copied into the sandbox at kernel:1010 and checked by `shim_test.c` section D. The sandbox's "Lua 5.2" comes from OC's own unmodified line (kernel:857, OC machine.lua:812) applied to the host's "Lua+Eris 5.2" (lj52shim.c:2757). The `persistKey`-nil (LuaJ) path is moot because eris persistence ships through OC's own `PersistenceAPI` and its `persistKey` (LuaJITArchitecture.java:384-465; save/restore in game T1–T3), on the GTNH kernel as patched by `native/kernel/patch-machine-lua.lua`.
- [x] **Value disposal (`__gc`)**: LuaJIT finalizes userdata and cdata, never tables, so OC's only route to `Value.dispose()` -- a `__gc` on machine.lua's TABLE proxy -- is severed. Fix in OUR native push path, not the serializer or the machine.lua fork: give the Value userdata (which already exists, behind the proxy) a Value-specific metatable whose C `__gc` calls `dispose` before releasing the global ref. ~15 lines; serializer cost zero. Intern Values at the push site (JNLua does not, so stock OC can double-dispose today). Note this bug does not exist until we ship: on stock OC table `__gc` fires and disposal works, so it is one we would INTRODUCE -- which is why it belongs on v1, the first build that could manifest it. Mechanism (g) subsumes the AE2 `NetworkControl` case by restoring the general route; a belt-and-braces dispose on the machine-stopped hook would be deliberately redundant, and lives in **OpenComputers' own `NetworkControl.scala`, not ours** -- an upstream patch or a fork edit, not a task on this list. It is called out only because it is the single leak whose consequences are permanent and compounding rather than bounded by a power cycle. See [../docs/research/gc-dispose-leak.md](research/gc-dispose-leak.md). **Status 2026-10-03:** still open, and "does not exist until we ship" needs a date: the leak is in every jar built since packaging (2026-09-16), the ones run in game (T1-T9) included. Nothing has been released, so the v1 reasoning stands. Nothing can fail on it yet. A milestone that must fail on HEAD and pass after the fix: open 16 handles (`Settings.maxHandles`) directly through the filesystem component, drop them unclosed, collect, and require a 17th open to succeed ([research/gc-dispose-leak.md](research/gc-dispose-leak.md):40). **Status 2026-10-10:** the user's GTNH pack sets `maxHandles=64` (OC's and ocelot-brain's default is 16, application.conf:926; verified 2026-10-10), so in that pack it takes 64 dropped handles to break `open` until reboot, not 16; the AE2 listener leak does not depend on it (NetworkControl.scala:611, :634-640). The pack also sets `allowGC=true`, under which stock runs user-defined table `__gc` (OC machine.lua:780-802): [research/gc-dispose-leak.md](research/gc-dispose-leak.md):31 counts that loss as zero only because OC ships `allowGC=false`, and this row's fix does not reach it, since LuaJIT finalizes no table. **Status 2026-10-13: done.** The results are in [bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md](../bench/results-dispose-2026-10-13.md), the evidence in [bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/](../bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/). Built in our layer as this row says, with no serializer or kernel line changed, but not as it describes: three of its claims are wrong, two found in source before the build (the archive's `design/design.md`, F1 and F3) and the third measured after it. **Not a Value-specific metatable** (F1): jnlua recognises a Java object only when its metatable is `rawequal` to `registry["jnlua.Object"]` (OC-JNLua jnlua.c:2093-2094), so a Value given any other metatable would no longer read back through `toJavaObjectRaw`, which OC's own `userdata.dispose` binding calls (UserdataAPI.scala:82). **No interning, and stock does not dispose a Value twice for being pushed twice** (F3): every push does mint a new userdata and global ref, but the kernel's `wrapSingleUserdata` compares `v == data` (OC machine.lua:1174) through JNLua's `__eq` on that shared metatable, so a Value re-pushed while its proxy lives lands on that EXISTING proxy and the new userdata is orphaned, finalized while the Value lives; one dispose per finalized userdata would dispose live Values. Harness `vd-1` (on a private state, through a reduced copy of the kernel's `wrapSingleUserdata`): a Value echoed back three times merges onto its live proxy 3 of 3, its three orphans are finalized with 0 disposes while the proxy lives, and 1 after. **Not ~15 lines** (`git diff --numstat` against aa12418, comments included): `native/lj52shim.c` +365, `lj52shim.h` +19, `gen-luastate-subclass.py` +493/-3 (which emits +394/-3 into `LuaStateLuaJIT.java`), `LuaJITArchitecture.java` +100, `build-native.sh` +62/-29, `build.gradle.kts` +20/-11; on the test side the new `vgc_test.c` (1 388 lines) and `run-vgc.sh`, `OcljSmoke.scala` +1 276/-3, `OcljArch.scala` +160/-2, `negative-control.sh` +137, `smoke-test.sh` +43. **What was built.** A FIELD replaced, not a metatable: `lj52_vgc_install` (`native/lj52shim.c:815`; the section THE VALUE FINALIZER, from `:616`) sets the `__gc` of `registry["jnlua.Object"]` to `lj52_vgc_gc` (`:743`), a C closure holding jnlua's own `gcjavaobject` as its upvalue: one `rawset`, so the field is never nil, stack-neutral under `lua_cpcall`, a second install keeping one wrapper level, every failure a code with no Java exception left pending (`lj52shim.h:273-276`). Per finalized userdata the wrapper makes no JNI call once `lj52_close` has set its closing flag (`:2527`); otherwise it takes the thread's JNIEnv, saves and clears a pending exception, and for an instance of the Value class (JavaFunction and LuaError userdata share the metatable and are filtered out) calls Java's `ocljValueFinalized` through a weak ref to the state, clears and counts an exception from it, re-throws the saved one, and then always chains to `gcjavaobject`, which releases the global ref as before. The count is Java's, an identity count in the generated `LuaStateLuaJIT` (`gen-luastate-subclass.py` `value_disposal()`, `:208`): live userdata per object, counted in `pushJavaObjectRaw` BEFORE the native push and rolled back if it throws (`LuaStateLuaJIT.java:224`), `pushJavaFunction` counted too when its function is also a Value, and not rolled back, because jnlua gives the userdata its metatable before it allocates the closure (OC-JNLua jnlua.c:656-660); the object is disposed when its last userdata is finalized (`ocljValueFinalized`, `:343`, not synchronized: a call without the monitor is counted and leaks rather than blocks); at zero, a live object of a class that overrides `equals` and equals it skips the dispose (a leak, never an early dispose); a dispose may not push into Lua; `close()` disposes nothing. `LuaJITArchitecture.initialize()` installs it right after `applyForinMode()`, before the kernel swap (`:159`; `installValueDisposal`, `:242`), with OC's own dispose body and log message (UserdataAPI.scala:83-85); a failed install is logged as an error and the machine starts anyway, as it did before disposal existed. `save()` runs a SECOND full `COLLECT` after OC's one, whether or not disposal installed (`:636`, after `:618`; the harness's twin the same, `OcljArch.scala:461`; F6): LuaJIT's weak-key tables are not ephemerons (lj_gc.c:214-217), so the raw userdata is finalized one full cycle after its proxy dies, and without the second collect a Value whose proxy died since the kernel's last collect would never be disposed at an unload save, since nothing is disposed at close. Gates: two new additive-only exports, `ocljInstallValueFinalizer` and `ocljValueGcCounters`, so the additive pins 91 `Java_*` / 93 PE names (was 89/91) and the dropin stays at 87/89 (`build-native.sh:904`, `:910`); the shim object must define `lj52_vgc_install` and `lj52_vgc_counters` in every build (`:651`); two preflight greps pin `"jnlua.Object"` in jnlua.c and in `lj52shim.h` (`:399-404`, `:405-409`); `verifyModAssets` requires all four oclj natives (`build.gradle.kts:163-168`); the generator refuses a `LuaState.java` that grows another `lua_pushjavaobject` or `lua_pushjavafunction` call site or changes the overridden signatures (`value_routes()`, `:185`). Shown failing: aa12418's DLL `45eb443b` reads oclj natives 2, `Java_*` 89, PE 91 and holds neither new name (`bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/logs/builds/exportgate-proof.txt`, whose .so check read a wrong path and printed nothing for aa12418's .so); aa12418's Linux .so `45b25422` reads oclj natives 2 and `Java_*` 89 and holds neither name, this build's `0deb53ab` 4 and 91 (`bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/logs/builds/exportgate-proof-so.txt`, `nm -D` in WSL); aa12418's `lj52shim.c` and `.h`, compiled again with the build's dropin line (object `79f19c63`, byte-identical to the 2026-10-05 reserve round's dropin shim object), define no `lj52_vgc_*` symbol, and `vgc_test` does not link against that object ("undefined reference to `lj52_vgc_counters'") while it reads 47/0 against this build's (`bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/logs/native/win/vgc-failfirst/`); the cross-check failed the two greps on altered copies and the object gates on the `build/native` shim object from before the change (not hashed). **Measured** (ocelot-brain on Windows 11, JDK 8; functional runs, not core-pinned; every JVM run loaded Windows DLLs, none the Linux .so). The milestone the 2026-10-03 status asked for is `gc-1` (live) and `gc-2` (after a restore), on a disk of their own: hold 16 handles (`maxHandles`) until refused, drop 15 keeping one bystander, then the filesystem's owners must fall and a fresh open succeed; `gc-3` drops 4 on another disk in one resume and saves at once, and the save itself must dispose them; `gc-0` checks the install against the configuration; `vd-1`..`vd-9` drive private states. Fail-first, the new harness on aa12418's natives and class (the harness as archived, but for one message edited before the post-fix runs, the stock arm's gc-0 SKIP text; no check, expectation or count changed): additive JIT on 79 checks, 13 failures, exactly gc-0..3 and vd-1..9 (gc-1 and gc-2: owners 16 → min 16, a fresh open refused `too_many_open_handles`; gc-3: owners 4 → 4), while dropin (66/0) and stock (50/0; stock disposes, owners 16 → min 1) pass. After, on additive DLL `6445f2e4` and dropin `60a8a522`: additive JIT on 79/0 (gc-1: owners 16 → min 1 → 0 after the bystander's close, disposer calls +15, fresh open ok; gc-3: owners 0 → 4 → 0 right after the save, 5 ms), JIT off 77/0, sieve 73/0, dropin 66/0, stock 50/0. CHECKS by arm (additive JIT on, dropin, additive JIT off, sieve, stock) 79/66/77/73/50, was 66/63/64/60/47: +13 on the additive arms (gc-0..3, vd-1..9), +3 on dropin and stock (gc-1..3; gc-0 and the vd milestones are SKIP there, not counted). Controls: `OCLJ_DISPOSE=off` 79/0 as the negative control (not installed, owners 16 → 16); `OCLJ_PERSIST_BUNDLE=off` 79/3, the three being stk-2/3/4, the failures that control exists to show, with the gc milestones run as `-NEGATIVE-CONTROL` because disposal never installs there. Sabotages: `save` with one `COLLECT` fails exactly gc-3 (owners 0 → 4 → 4); counting after the native push fails exactly vd-9 (a Value disposed by its own push). Native: `vgc_test` 47/0 on Windows and on Linux (WSL), no JVM, a strict fake JNIEnv; mem 86/0 (`_OCLJ_GCSTATS` still 20 values), wd 35/0 and penalty 6/0 on both (on Linux, C hosts over the LuaJIT and shim objects of the .so `0deb53ab`, which no JVM has loaded); on Windows shim 57/0, security 37/0 and race 2/0 (run against a mirror of the old build layout, which their scripts still name), and the negative control PASS, its ten new Value-finalizer sabotages each failing exactly its predicted set (52 `RESULT: PASS` lines across all its sections). `gradlew build` passes. One instrument incident: chain a's third batch, three JVMs started in the same second, all stopped at +12 s with "no native loaded", before anything under test ran, while its first two batches, also three JVMs started in one second, ran. The likely cause, not proven, is OC's bundled 5.2 native being extracted into the shared working directory by each JVM (two of the three logs read "Could not update native library"). Run one at a time, all three ran (the stopped attempts in `bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/logs/incident/`, the reruns in its `logs/suite/fix-bundleoff-b/` and `logs/sabotage/`; `logs/chain-a.txt`, `logs/chain-b.txt`). **Not covered, and where it differs from stock:** the product's `LuaJITArchitecture.java` is exercised by compile and spotless only -- the harness runs its twin, `OcljArch.scala`, as for every earlier row -- so its install and its second `COLLECT` have no in-game run, and there is no in-game test yet (the T10 row; the pack's `maxHandles=64`). Only filesystem `HandleValue`s and the harness's own counting Values are exercised: AE2's `NetworkContents` iterators (no AE2 in the harness) and internet sockets and requests are not; they go through the same `Value.dispose`. The Linux additive .so `0deb53ab` has been loaded by no JVM, so on Linux its finalizer's JNI upcall has run only against `vgc_test`'s fake JNIEnv (the row "The Linux native has never been loaded by a JVM"). Nothing is disposed at `lua_close`, which is stock parity (F5): jnlua zeroes the Java state before `lua_close` (OC-JNLua jnlua.c:358-364) and OC's binding calls `toJavaObjectRaw` outside its `try` (UserdataAPI.scala:81-88), so a Value alive at shutdown or unload leaks on stock too. Disposal lands one GC cycle later than on stock; the save's second `COLLECT` keeps "dead at the save is disposed", at one more full GC per machine per save, not measured beyond gc-3's 3-7 ms saves (unpinned) of a 186-241 KB `_kernel`; and neither the push path's cost (an identity-map step per Value push, an `isInstance` per other push) nor the finalizer's (a GetEnv, an ExceptionOccurred, an IsInstanceOf and a chained `lua_call` for every finalized Java-object userdata, Value or not, plus a JNI upcall and an identity-map step per Value userdata) is measured. A Value pushed but never wrapped (an error between push and wrap) is disposed; stock leaks it. A raw userdata left in a user table by the kernel's unwrapUserdata in-place rewrite keeps its Value alive until that table dies. Third-party Values that override `equals`: at zero, an equal live Value skips the dispose (a leak, never premature), and the sequential equal case can leak where stock disposes both (`vd-3` records a2=0 b2=1). User-defined table `__gc` (`allowGC=true`, which the user's pack sets) stays unsupported: LuaJIT finalizes no table. The dropin keeps the leak (it never ships), and `OCLJ_PERSIST_BUNDLE=off` never installs disposal, because OC's two-call save mints a second `HandleValue`, with the same handle id, for every proxy its second blob reaches (every proxy, when the save lands mid-sync-call), and disposing that copy would close the live file (F10; [shell-fill.md](shell-fill.md):381-383). The new raw global `_OCLJ_VGCSTATS` is a new eris permanent outside the blob fingerprint: the cross-build blob compatibility row. Corrections to [research/gc-dispose-leak.md](research/gc-dispose-leak.md) are at its top; they moved its lines, so the `:40` and `:31` cited by the 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-10 statuses above are now `:137` and `:128`.
- [x] Component-call protocol: direct calls + `LimitReachedException` → zero results → machine.lua's synchronized-call fallback; `runSynchronized` closure resume **Done -- verified 2026-10-03:** Inherited rather than written. `LuaJITArchitecture` extends OC's `NativeLuaArchitecture` (LuaJITArchitecture.java:83) and overrides neither `runThreaded` nor `runSynchronized`. OC's `invoke` turns `LimitReachedException` into zero results (OC NativeLuaArchitecture.scala:59-77), and the kernel's `invoke`, which no patch site touches, falls back to `coroutine.yield(closure)` (build/native/kernel/machine.lua:1117-1131). Exercised end to end by harness `stk-1..4`: a burst of eight `fs.open`s past the 4-per-tick limit, saved and restored inside `SynchronizedCall`, then the restored closure run (OcljSmoke.scala:943, main flow :5479; 42/0 with the bundle override). In game, T2 passed on 2026-09-19.
- [x] Simple wall-clock watchdog (soft error → hard state-kill after resume returns) — sufficient while interpreter-only, since hooks always fire in the interpreter **Superseded -- 2026-10-03:** The interpreter-only premise ended when the JIT was turned on, and what shipped is the prototype's asynchronous design (the v2 adopt-the-watchdog row), not a simple wall clock. There is no standing hook. A per-machine native timer (a Win32 timer queue, or a Linux `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` condvar thread) ORs the count bit into `hookmask` on the CHECKHOOK build and re-fires every 50 ms until disarm (native/lj52shim.c:1472, :1585, arm/disarm :1850-1940). It is armed at kernel sites machine.lua:751/:883/:1625. Measured by `wd_test` (32/32 on both platforms 2026-09-16, 35/0 at the 10-03 gates), by harness `k1-deadline-still-fires`, `k5-watchdog-fired` and `k6-grace-expiry-crashes-cleanly`, and in game by T4: a pcall-swallowed timeout past its grace stops the machine with `too long without yielding` (PASS 2026-09-22). What remains is the hard abort for a resume wedged in native code, which is v2's hard-abort row.
- [ ] Native build pipeline: pinned LuaJIT v2.1 commit, `LUA52COMPAT` + **`CHECKHOOK`**, static single-lib shim; win-x64 + linux-x64 first, extraction/probe loader cloned from `LuaStateFactory`. **CHECKHOOK is not optional once the JIT is on, and the reason is stronger than timeout enforcement: OC's kernel does not BOOT without it.** `machine.lua`'s first executable statement is `calcHookInterval()`, which spins `while bogomipsBusy do ... end` until a count hook clears the flag; on a stock build that loop compiles to a trace, the hook never fires, and the machine hangs in mcode during sandbox construction. Measured: hang with stock, every milestone green with CHECKHOOK, nothing else changed. Unplanned bonus — that loop is a live, in-kernel, end-to-end regression test for the watchdog's central mechanism. **Status 2026-10-03:** Built and asserted: the `LUA52COMPAT` + `CHECKHOOK` flags (build-native.sh:242), checked on the compile line (:520-523) and by behaviour (:532-555); static LuaJIT plus jnlua, shim and eris linked into one library (:779-782); win-x64 and linux-x64 artifacts, both in `build/native/dist` (`wd_test` 32/32 on each); and the loader, inherited rather than cloned (`LuaJITStateFactory extends LuaStateFactory`, `version()` = `"jit52"`). So the :275 grep cannot reject a stock v2.1 clone, and the probe at :539-555 is the real gate. **Also open, from the 2026-10-03 review:** the pin cannot hold on the build copy either, because `luajit-$PLATFORM` is copied from the checkout only when it is absent (`:419-424`) and only `lj_func.c` is re-copied on every build (`:447`), so a copy made from an older checkout survives a checkout change.
